Sell-side estimates

❌ D463 questions
C-6748C-6946C-7450

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell-side on Storskogen is thin (two visible brokers, one of which writes the narrative for the whole peer group), divided (30% apart on 2026 EPS) and has spent a year cutting (FY2026 EPS down 18.5% over twelve months, FY2027 down 13%, target prices down 11% in three months) while raising its buy share, a divergence that the dashboard's D rating and negative 2 momentum score correctly flag. The cuts have now stopped. The forecasts themselves are sensibly shaped: flat EBITDA margins in 2026, a slow margin repair to about 10% by 2028, and an EPS recovery (plus 8% in 2026, plus 21% in 2027) driven mostly by the executed interest saving and by operating leverage on a fixed amortisation charge, with modest acquisition contribution and no credit for the 15% target. The record against these numbers is small beats in the strong quarters and misses in the weak, and consistent misses against the company's own targets. For the model: take consensus EBITA as the base, lean the 2026 EPS toward DNB Carnegie's SEK 1.11 rather than the SEK 1.03 median (the Nordea number looks stale), treat 2027 as the year of greatest exposure in both directions (Services, deals, STIBOR), and expect each quarter to move the outer years by more than at guided peers because there is no company anchor.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, line by line, and the credibility of the gap

❌ D48

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement: the forecasts are modest on the operating lines and aggressive on the lines below operating profit, which is the right shape for this company and makes the headline EPS growth look better founded than it is. Line by line (consensus median, two brokers; history on the brokers' basis where stated):

  • Revenue: SEK 34,219m in 2026 (plus 3.4%), SEK 36,022m in 2027 (plus 5.3%), SEK 37,309m in 2028 (plus 3.6%), against plus 5.1% in 2023, negative 5.1% in 2024, negative 3.2% in 2025; the forecasts assume 3% to 4% organic plus 1% to 2% acquired in 2026 and roughly 4% plus 1% to 2% in 2027 with currency neutral. Credible; if anything light on acquisitions in 2027 to 2028 if the cadence rises, and heavy if the krona strengthens further.
  • EBITDA margin (brokers' basis): 12.2%, 12.2%, 12.4% against 12.5% in 2025 (SEK 4,144m), 12.8% in 2024, 11.9% in 2023; essentially flat, which assumes Industry's gain offsets Services' and Trade's drag. Credible and possibly conservative for 2027 if Services recovers.
  • EBIT margin (after amortisation): 7.0%, 7.7%, 8.2% against 6.2% in 2025, 5.9% in 2024, 5.5% in 2023; this implies amortisation and depreciation of about SEK 1.8bn flat while EBITDA grows, so the EBIT margin expansion is mostly operating leverage on a fixed amortisation charge plus the divested losses rolling off. Credible for 2026 to 2027; 8.2% in 2028 requires a 10%-plus EBITA margin.
  • Net financial items: implied about SEK 606m in 2026 (EBIT SEK 2,380m less pre-tax SEK 1,774m), SEK 560m in 2027, SEK 530m in 2028, against SEK 828m in 2025 and SEK 1,044m in 2024; this is the single largest driver of the 2026 to 2027 EPS growth and is well founded on executed refinancing, with a risk from STIBOR and the late-2027 refinancing.
  • Tax: implied about 26% (net income SEK 1,309m on pre-tax SEK 1,774m), against 24.4% reported in 2025; reasonable.
  • EPS (adjusted, brokers' basis): SEK 1.03 (plus 8%), SEK 1.25 (plus 21%), SEK 1.22 (negative 2%, a median artefact; DNB Carnegie SEK 1.36, Nordea SEK 1.22) against SEK 0.953 in 2025 (plus 10%), SEK 0.867 in 2024 (plus 13%), SEK 0.767 in 2023. The 2027 jump is the full-year interest saving plus margin recovery plus M&A it is the year where the forecast is most exposed.
  • Share count: flat at about 1,687m; no buyback assumed beyond the SEK 100m done.
  • Cash flow: operating cash flow SEK 2,829m, SEK 3,161m, SEK 3,236m (against SEK 2,451m in 2025, SEK 3,098m in 2024); capex SEK 613m to SEK 656m (1.8%); free cash flow SEK 1,541m, SEK 2,087m, SEK 2,410m (against SEK 1,732m); dividend per share SEK 0.13, SEK 0.145, SEK 0.16.
  • Balance sheet: equity SEK 21.6bn to SEK 24.8bn; leverage (DNB Carnegie) 2.4x in 2026, 2.2x in 2027, which implies the brokers assume less acquisition spend than the company's rhetoric.

The credibility judgement: the operating forecasts are consistent with a cyclical stabilisation and a slow margin repair, the financial forecasts are consistent with executed actions, and the gap between them (EBITDA up 12% from 2025 to 2028 while EPS rises about 30% to 40%) is explained by interest, amortisation leverage and tax. The two places where I would lean the model are: Nordea's 2026 EPS (SEK 0.77) looks too low against the H1 run-rate (SEK 0.36 adjusted in a seasonally weak half); DNB Carnegie's 2028 (SEK 1.36) assumes an acquisition contribution not yet evidenced. The forecasts do not embed the 15% target and do not embed a Services recovery to 11%, which are the upside risks.

C-69

Record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, including in comparable conditions

❌ D46

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

Judgement: against consensus, an alternating record skewed to small beats in the strong quarters and misses in the weak ones; against its own targets, consistent misses. The last eight quarters: Q3 2024 beat (EBITA 8% above consensus); Q4 2024 beat (adjusted EBITA up 20% year on year, DNB Carnegie raised forecasts 3%); Q1 2025 weak (DNB Carnegie credit note "weak Q1"); Q2 2025 "actually not so bad"; Q3 2025 beat (SEK 759m against SEK 737m, about 3%); Q4 2025 in line with FactSet and 2% above Infront; Q1 2026 miss (adjusted EBITA 3% below Modular Finance consensus, 6% below DNB Carnegie; sales 1% to 2% below); Q2 2026 beat (sales and EBITA both 2% above). Over three years the pattern is beats of 2% to 8% in Q2 to Q4 and misses in Q1, driven by weather and construction, with the sell-side repeatedly cutting outer-year estimates after each quarter regardless (FY2026 EPS consensus down 18.5% over twelve months). Over five years: the 2021 listing targets (above 10% margin, 15% growth) have been missed every year; 2025 delivered 9.4% and negative 3%. In comparable conditions (the 2023 to 2024 construction downturn and the 2022 rate shock), the company missed on margin and leverage and delivered on cash; the cash record is the one consistent beat.

For the model: the sell-side's quarterly numbers are beatable by small amounts in Q2 and Q4 and missable in Q1 and Q3; the outer-year numbers have been too high for a year and are only now stabilising (one-month revisions flat).

C-74

Extent to which estimates have been cut deeply and fully across all analysts; has guidance been rebased, kitchen-sinked or sandbagged

C50

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

Judgement: estimates have been cut deeply (FY2026 consensus EPS down 18.5% over twelve months and 11.7% over six, FY2027 down 12.9%, FY2028 down 15.2%; for the 2027 year EPS down 13% and EBITDA down 4% over twelve months while sales rose 3%, so the cuts are margin and below-the-line, not volume), gradually rather than in one reset, and they have now stopped (one month: 0% to plus 0.6%; three months: negative 1% to negative 1.3%). The target price has been cut 11% over three months (DNB Carnegie from SEK 18.0 to SEK 15.0 in four steps in 2026, the last on 20 October 2026 on lower peer multiples and a more cautious Services view); the buy share rose to 80% (plus 20 points over three months) as a broker added or re-rated, which the Wisdom Library's "buy rating against revisions divergence" pattern flags as a composite that can mask component cuts. Across all analysts: both visible brokers have cut; whether the cuts are "full" cannot be known with two contributors, and the 30% gap between them says one of them is wrong. Guidance: not rebased (there is none), not kitchen-sinked (the targets were retained), not sandbagged (the targets are above consensus). The dashboard's sell-side momentum rating is D and its revision-momentum score negative 2.

The reading for the model: the estimate cycle looks to have bottomed on the numbers (flat one-month revisions, a 2% Q2 beat, a target cut driven by peer multiples rather than by estimates), which is the point at which a restarted engine starts generating the unpriced upgrades the sector pattern describes, provided Q3 on 23 October does not restart the cuts.

Stock market narrative

❌ D461 question
C-2248

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative available to Storskogen over the next three years is a recovery narrative with a ceiling: "repaired balance sheet, engine restarting, cheap against peers", with dated proofs (Q3 on 23 October, the February 2027 full-year report, the quarterly deal announcements, the late-2027 refinancing) and undated ones (a permanent Services head, two consecutive quarters of positive organic EBITA, a 10% margin). The sector's own research this month puts Storskogen at the front of the cohort the market is expected to pay for in a short-cycle turn, and the mechanism (consensus models only owned businesses, so each deal is an upgrade) is well founded and already visible in the stabilisation of estimate revisions. What the narrative cannot offer is anything structurally new: no moat, no step-change in returns, no capital-return programme, no guidance to raise, and a target set that will be missed at its first checkpoint. The Consumer should model the narrative's effect on the multiple as a move from about 9x toward 10x to 12x EV/EBITA over 12 to 24 months if the proofs arrive (DNB Carnegie's SEK 15 target is 12x 2026 and 10x 2027), and a move back toward 7x to 8x if Q3 or Q1 2027 restart the estimate cuts; the fundamental narrative does not support peer multiples at any point in the horizon.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments ahead: next 6 months, months 6 to 18, months 18 to 36; novelty and foundation

❌ D48

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Judgement: the staircase is real but its steps are low and few are dated; the narrative the market can buy is "the balance sheet is fixed and the engine is restarting", and the steps are the proofs of that.

Next 6 months (to April 2027): (1) Q3 2026 on 23 October 2026, a seasonally weak quarter where the market will look for organic EBITA turning positive, Services margin stability and the deal cadence; novelty low, foundation strong (dated). (2) Further acquisitions "quarter by quarter", with Duratec and Hawedo just announced; each is an unpriced upgrade of perhaps 0.3% to 0.5% of EBITA; novelty moderate (the market has not yet seen a rising cadence), foundation moderate. (3) A permanent Head of Services; novelty moderate, foundation weak (no date). (4) The full-year 2025 report in February 2027 with the dividend (consensus SEK 0.13) and the 2026 leverage outcome; dated. (5) The Riksbank decision in November 2026, a negative step the market must climb past. (6) The krona procurement tailwind reaching Trade margins in Q3 and Q4; foundation moderate.

Months 6 to 18 (to October 2027 or April 2028): (1) Two consecutive quarters of positive organic EBITA growth, the risk report's progression trigger; novelty high for this stock (it has not happened since 2024), foundation moderate (Industry's cycle supports it; Services does not yet). (2) A Services margin above 10% for a full year; novelty high, foundation weak. (3) Acquired sales contribution reaching the 5% a year DNB Carnegie models, visible in reported growth outrunning organic; novelty moderate, foundation moderate. (4) The late-2027 refinancing executed at a lower spread, possibly with a rating upgrade (Moody's B1, S&P BB stable); novelty moderate, foundation moderate. (5) A 2026 full-year adjusted EPS above SEK 1.10 and 2027 consensus holding at SEK 1.25; foundation moderate. (6) Consensus beginning to model acquisitions, which the sector pattern says is the mechanism of re-rating.

Months 18 to 36 (to October 2028 or 2029): (1) The 2025 to 2027 target period closing with a verdict (the 15% growth target will almost certainly be missed; the 10% margin may be met in 2027 or 2028), and a new set of targets or a Capital Markets Day, which is the one genuinely novel narrative event; foundation weak (none scheduled). (2) Leverage approaching the 2.5x ceiling again after two years of deployment, which is the sector pattern's end of the window; a negative step. (3) A larger platform acquisition or a non-Nordic platform (the UK) reaching scale; novelty high, foundation weak. (4) Evidence that the 2026 to 2027 vintage of deals earns its 7x price (no impairments, earn-outs met); foundation unknowable today.

What is missing from the staircase, and why the narrative is capped: no large buyback, no break-up or disposal of a whole business area, no visible path to peer-level returns, no guidance that could be raised, and a founder-controlled structure that removes the take-out floor. The novel positive the market can price in the next 18 months is a rising deal count with organic EBITA turning positive; that is well founded on the Industry cycle and the cash available, and it is exactly what the "newly lit acquirer" cohort is being bought for this month. The negative steps (Riksbank, Q3 seasonality, the 2027 refinancing, the window closing) are as well founded.

Company: Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), a Swedish serial acquirer and owner of about 114 small and medium-sized businesses in three business areas: Industry (industrial technology, automation and product solutions; SEK 14,319m of 2025 net sales), Trade (business-to-business distribution and brand owners in professional products, health and beauty and consumer products; SEK 9,588m) and Services (infrastructure services exposed to construction, and business services including digital and logistics; SEK 9,232m); group net sales SEK 33,097m and adjusted EBITA SEK 3,117m (9.4% margin) in 2025 (annual report, April 2026), listed on Nasdaq Stockholm since 2021.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Lifco AB (LIFCO.B-SE), the Swedish decentralised serial acquirer with the highest margins in the cohort and the cleanest record of margin behaviour through the 2009 and 2020 downturns; Indutrade AB (INDT-SE), a Swedish technology and industrial serial acquirer with a long record of organic margin behaviour across cycles; Addtech AB (ADDT.B-SE), a Swedish technology trading group that reported a record 16% EBITA margin in its 2025/26 fiscal year on flat organic sales; Sdiptech AB (SDIP.B-SE), a Swedish infrastructure serial acquirer that completed a programme of eleven divestments in 2025 to 2026 and reports a core adjusted EBITA margin around 21%; Volati AB (VOLO-SE), a Swedish serial acquirer whose construction and agriculture platforms show the fixed-cost under-absorption that a volume fall produces; Indus Holding AG (INH-DE), a German Mittelstand holding with a comparable buy-and-hold model and three segments; private competitors in Nordic infrastructure and installation services (for example Bravida and Instalco as listed references for the Services business area's construction-exposed units) and in Nordic professional distribution. How did adjusted EBITA margins move with volume in each business area in the 2023 to 2025 downturn and in the first half of 2026 (for example the Services area's adjusted EBITA falling 30% to SEK 166m on net sales of SEK 2,147m in the first quarter of 2026 per the Q1 2026 interim report, and the Industry area's margin rising to 11.0% from 10.4% in the second quarter of 2026 per the Q2 2026 interim report), and what incremental margin on volume does that history imply for each area, in particular for Infrastructure Services and for the Product Solutions vertical in Industry where management has reported low capacity utilisation? Focus: What most needs answering is how much adjusted EBITA the Services and Industry business areas would add on a volume recovery of a few percentage points given the fixed costs now carried in construction-exposed and under-utilised units, and, conversely, how much they would lose in a renewed downturn, so that the margin path from 9.4% toward the company's 10% target can be built from drop-through rather than assumed.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Lifco AB (LIFCO.B-SE), whose former executives have described mandated post-acquisition price increases and whose dental software units raise prices annually; Indutrade AB (INDT-SE) and Addtech AB (ADDT.B-SE), Swedish technology trading groups that passed through the 2021 to 2023 input-cost inflation and whose reports describe the lag; Sdiptech AB (SDIP.B-SE), which has disclosed a six-to-nine-month lag between input-cost inflation and price pass-through in some units and multi-year framework agreements in others; Volati AB (VOLO-SE), whose Tornum and S:t Eriks platforms have reported price discounting in volume downturns; Indus Holding AG (INH-DE), whose BETEK subsidiary passed through a very large tungsten price rise in 2025 to 2026; private and listed competitors of Storskogen's construction-exposed Infrastructure Services units in Sweden and Denmark, and distributors competing with the Trade area's professional products and health and beauty units in the Nordics. Focus: What most needs answering is whether Storskogen's units can hold price when volume falls in construction and consumer niches and whether the Industry area's 2026 price increases are cost recovery or real pricing, so that the revenue and margin forecast can separate price from volume and assign a pass-through lag by business area.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: The investment case now rests on a feedback loop (cash flow funds acquisitions at or below 7x EBITA, which raise EBITA, which lowers leverage and funds more acquisitions, while consensus adds each deal only when it closes) and on a possible opposite loop (negative organic EBITA, rising amortisation, minority buyouts and earn-outs consuming cash, and a rising unit count without an internal audit function), and the second-set Analyst needs the evidence on which loop is operating and how far it can run. Peers: Lifco AB (LIFCO.B-SE), the cohort's reference for a self-funded acquisition loop that has run for a decade at about 6x to 8x EBITA and low leverage; Sdiptech AB (SDIP.B-SE), the closest recent precedent for a reset followed by a restart of acquisitions after divestments and deleveraging in 2025 to 2026; Volati AB (VOLO-SE), the control case where leverage after a spin-off stopped the acquisition loop in 2026; Indus Holding AG (INH-DE), a German buy-and-hold holding with a stated EUR 500m acquisition programme to 2030; Indutrade AB (INDT-SE) and Addtech AB (ADDT.B-SE), Swedish serial acquirers whose acquisition pace continued through the 2023 to 2025 downturn; Röko AB and Teqnion AB as smaller Swedish acquirers whose control and head-office capacity have been tested by growth. Inputs: The positive loop: free cash flow after dividends, earn-outs and minority buyouts available for acquisitions each year (Storskogen reported cash flow from operating activities of SEK 2,451m in 2025 per the 2025 annual report; its stated long-term target is adjusted cash conversion above 70%; its leverage target is 2.0x to 3.0x interest-bearing net debt to adjusted EBITDA with the chief financial officer stating an ambition to stay below 2.5x on the Q1 2026 call); the acquired EBITA that cash buys at the company's stated ceiling of 7x EBITA; the resulting EBITA growth and leverage path; the sell-side's practice of adding acquisitions to estimates only on completion (DNB Carnegie has stated that acquisitions are not normally reflected in consensus); how long such a loop ran at Lifco, Indutrade and Addtech and what stopped it at Storskogen in 2022 to 2023 (leverage of about 4.35x at the 2022 peak per DNB Carnegie) and at Volati in 2026. The negative loop: amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 175m in the second quarter of 2026 per the Q2 2026 presentation) rising with each deal; minority put option liabilities (SEK 1,740m at mid-2026 per the Q2 2026 interim report) and contingent considerations consuming cash; goodwill as a share of purchase price in 2026 combinations (about 78% per the Q2 2026 interim report); the 2024 impairment of SEK 920m on nine divested units; the absence of an internal audit function across the units (per the 2025 corporate governance report); whether organic EBITA has grown or shrunk in the units acquired in 2020 to 2022 since acquisition; and whether a rising unit count has historically preceded control failures at Storskogen (the Tungdykargruppen charge of SEK 30m in 2020) and at peers. The cyclical loop: whether the Industry area's order intake and capacity utilisation reinforce each other in an upturn, and whether construction weakness in Services, fixed costs and price competition reinforce each other in a downturn, with dated precedents at Storskogen and at Volati. Focus: What most needs answering is how many years the positive acquisition loop can run from a 2.4x starting point before leverage reaches 2.5x to 3.0x again, at what annual acquisition spend and EBITA contribution, and what evidence from Storskogen's own 2020 to 2022 vintage and from the peers shows whether acquired units' earnings hold or decay after purchase.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: Storskogen's consensus earnings per share growth over 2026 to 2028 comes mostly from lines below operating profit (net financial items, amortisation, tax) and from an acquisition contribution that neither the company nor the two covering brokers quantify, and the two brokers differ by about 30% on 2026 earnings per share, so the second-set Analyst needs the inputs to each line of the bridge rather than a consensus total. Peers: Lifco AB (LIFCO.B-SE), Indutrade AB (INDT-SE), Addtech AB (ADDT.B-SE) and Sdiptech AB (SDIP.B-SE) as Swedish serial acquirers whose reports disclose amortisation of acquired intangibles, net financial items, tax rates and minority and earn-out liabilities on a comparable basis; Volati AB (VOLO-SE) as a Swedish peer with floating-rate debt and disclosed interest sensitivity; Indus Holding AG (INH-DE) as a German peer with swapped floating-rate liabilities and a disclosed free-cash-flow bridge. Inputs: (1) Net financial items: the composition and run-rate after the September 2025 refinancing of the 2027 bond into a 2029 bond and the 2025/2030 bond of SEK 1,000m (per the November 2025 prospectus), the interest-rate swaps outstanding (SEK 3,899m nominal at the end of 2025 per the 2025 annual report, of which SEK 2,000m expiring within a year), the floating-rate share of net debt and its sensitivity to a 100 basis point move in STIBOR, the timing and likely terms of the late-2027 bank and bond maturities, and the credit-rating path (Moody's B1 since March 2023; S&P BB with the outlook moved to stable). (3) Tax: the reported rate (24.4% in 2025 per the 2025 annual report, 28.5% in 2023) and the durability of the interest-deduction structure introduced in 2025 (per the Q3 2025 call) under Swedish interest-limitation rules. (4) Minority interests, put option liabilities (SEK 1,740m at mid-2026) and contingent considerations: the cash schedule of buyouts and earn-outs in 2026 to 2028 and their effect on free cash flow available for acquisitions.
