Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

The company reports three business areas (net sales and adjusted EBITA). 2025 actuals: Industry SEK 14,319m (43%), Trade SEK 9,588m (29%, adjusted EBITA SEK 831m, 8.7%), Services SEK 9,232m (28%); group adjusted EBITA SEK 3,117m (9.4%). The 2025 Industry and Services margins are not in the files read; my estimates are Industry about 10.6% and Services about 10.0%, with central costs of about SEK 155m to SEK 165m, which reconcile to the group figure. Latest quarter (Q2 2026): Industry SEK 3,967m at 11.0%; Services about SEK 2,579m at 10.3% (7.7% in Q1 2026); Trade SEK 2,315m at 9.2% (7.1% in Q1 2026).

My base case by segment (sales, adjusted EBITA margin):

  • Industry: FY2026 SEK 14.8bn, 10.9%; FY2027 SEK 15.7bn, 11.2%; FY2028 SEK 17.0bn, 11.4%; FY2029 SEK 18.4bn, 11.4%; FY2030 SEK 19.8bn, 11.4%. Organic growth 5% in H2 2026, 4% in H1 2027, 3% thereafter; acquired growth 1% in 2026, 3% to 5% from 2027; Product Solutions flat until 2027.
  • Trade: FY2026 SEK 9.2bn (-3.6%: Perfect Hair and Motavo divested, about SEK 560m of sales; consumer destocking; krona), 8.9%; FY2027 SEK 9.8bn, 9.5%; FY2028 SEK 10.5bn, 9.6%; FY2029 SEK 11.2bn, 9.5%; FY2030 SEK 11.9bn, 9.5%. The 2026 to 2027 margin gain is largely the unhedged krona lowering euro and dollar purchase costs with a two-to-four-quarter lag; I do not let it build into the long run.
  • Services: FY2026 SEK 9.6bn, 9.0%; FY2027 SEK 10.2bn, 9.6%; FY2028 SEK 11.0bn, 10.2%; FY2029 SEK 12.0bn, 10.5%; FY2030 SEK 13.0bn, 10.5%. Organic growth 2% in 2026, 2.5% in 2027, 3% thereafter; acquired growth 1.5% in 2026 (Safi), 3% to 5% from 2027. The margin path assumes a permanent head of Services by early 2027, construction volumes stabilising in H2 2027 and 50 to 100 basis points from standardisation and selective bidding over two years. This is the most exposed line in the model.
  • Central costs: SEK 170m to SEK 180m a year, flat in nominal terms.
  • Group adjusted EBITA: FY2026 SEK 3,130m (9.3%), FY2027 SEK 3,433m (9.6%), FY2028 SEK 3,826m (9.9%), FY2029 SEK 4,178m (10.0%), FY2030 SEK 4,541m (10.1%). Group revenue: SEK 33,770m, 35,897m, 38,769m, 41,870m, 45,011m.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

Revenue growth. FY2026 +2.0%: organic +3.5% (price about 2%, volume about 1.5%), acquired +1.5% (Darlington, Safi, Verdant, Duratec, Hawedo and add-ons, about SEK 650m of annual sales), divestments -1.7% (Perfect Hair, Motavo), currency -1.3% (a stronger krona on 55% of sales outside Sweden; EUR/SEK toward 10.50 on Nordea's path). H1 2026 actual was +2.0%; Q3 +1.8% and Q4 +2.4% follow from organic growth of about 3% (Q3 is seasonally weak with "customer restraint" flagged in Trade), acquired growth rising from 2% to 2.5% as the July and October deals consolidate, and currency drag deepening into Q4. FY2027 +6.3%: organic +3.5%, acquired +4% (half-year effect of SEK 1.6bn of new deals at 7x EBITA on a 10% to 12% margin, about SEK 1.4bn of sales per SEK 1bn spent, plus the carry-over of the 2026 deals), currency -1%, divestments -0.2%. FY2028 and FY2029 +8.0%, FY2030 +7.5%: organic 3%, acquired 5.5% (spend of SEK 1.9bn, 2.1bn, 2.3bn), currency neutral. Acquired growth is the main difference from consensus, which does not model uncompleted acquisitions.

EBITDA margin (brokers' basis: adjusted EBITA plus depreciation excluding acquired intangibles, after IFRS 16). Built as adjusted EBITA margin plus depreciation at 3.0% to 3.1% of sales. The EBITA margin rises from 9.3% to 10.1% through: Industry pricing and utilisation (+10 to +20 basis points over three years), Services recovery and productivity (+30 to +40 basis points), Trade currency and B2B mix (+10 to +20 basis points, partly reversing), acquired units at 10% to 12% margins (+10 to +15 basis points), pruning of loss-makers (+10 basis points in 2026), against Services price pressure and wage inflation in 2026 and ERP and compliance costs. Operating leverage on volume is 25% to 35% in Services and Product Solutions and 12% to 20% elsewhere, worth SEK 100m to SEK 150m over 2027 to 2028; there is no group cost programme. Quarterly seasonality: Q1 weakest (EBITA margin about 8.1% to 8.4%), Q3 weak (9.5%), Q2 and Q4 strong (9.7% to 10.0%); Part 3 adds 3.0% to 3.1% of depreciation to each.

Depreciation (excluding acquired intangibles). 2025: SEK 1,027m (3.1% of sales), including right-of-use asset depreciation. Held at 3.0% to 3.1% of sales as acquired units bring fixed assets and leases. Amortisation of acquired intangibles, excluded here and from adjusted EPS: about SEK 690m in 2026 rising to SEK 740m, 810m, 880m, 950m (about SEK 56m per SEK 1bn deployed, net of run-off).

Net financial items. H1 2026 SEK 277m (SEK 420m a year earlier) after the 2025 refinancings (SEK 1bn 2025/2030 bond at STIBOR plus 265 basis points; the 2027 bond refinanced into 2029). H2 2026 about SEK 303m including a November 2026 Riksbank hike on roughly SEK 6bn to SEK 7bn of unhedged floating debt (company sensitivity SEK 69m per 100 basis points) and a small allowance for FX items. FY2027 SEK 575m: full-year hike effect offset by the late-2027 refinancing of Bond 1 (SEK 1,250m at STIBOR plus 375) at a lower spread. FY2028 to FY2030 rising to SEK 650m as net debt grows SEK 0.6bn to SEK 0.8bn a year with acquisitions, at a blended cost of about 5.5% to 6%. Includes lease interest. No rating upgrade assumed.

Tax rate. 24.4% reported in 2025 after an internal restructuring improved interest deductibility; 25.5% in 2026 (the SEK 65m Perfect Hair loss is non-deductible); 25% thereafter, which is above the 22.8% to 23.8% of peers. Risk to 27% to 28% if Swedish interest-limitation rules change or a further divestment loss arises.

Dividend per share. Policy 15% of net earnings; SEK 0.11 paid for 2025 (about 17% of reported profit). My reported net income of about SEK 1,270m, 1,530m, 1,760m, 1,950m, 2,140m gives SEK 0.12, 0.14, 0.16, 0.18, 0.20 (declared for each year, paid the following spring).

Share count. About 1,678m shares outstanding after the SEK 100m buyback of 8,865,000 B shares completed in January 2026 (SEK 10m of it falling in 2026). The share savings programme is cash-settled; 8.6m C1 hurdle shares convert only if price hurdles are met by June 2029 (not assumed). No further buybacks in the base case despite a SEK 1,337m authorisation, because management's stated priority is acquisitions.

Capital spending. SEK 658m in 2025 (2.0% of sales including intangibles); SEK 343m in H1 2026 with Q2 at 2.3% on Industry growth projects; 2.1% of sales through the horizon (above Lifco and Addtech at 0.8%, in line with Indutrade at 1.8%).

Working capital. FactSet's year-end definition (receivables plus inventories less payables over trailing revenue) was 23.4% at end-2025 and 22.7% at end-2024, against 18% to 19% in the three other quarters; the company's narrower measure is about 15%. I ease the year-end ratio from 23.2% to 22.0% over five years because acquired units' working capital is bought with the deal rather than built, and because the company is targeting lower working capital. The ratio implies a build of SEK 350m to SEK 600m a year, which is why free cash flow grows more slowly than EBITDA. The seasonal pattern (Q2 build, H2 release) should be applied to any quarterly cash forecast.

Lease payments. FactSet's total debt (SEK 11,343m at June 2026) exceeds the company's interest-bearing liabilities by about SEK 1.6bn of lease liabilities; annual lease payments of about SEK 480m rising with the unit count.

Acquisitions less disposals. Includes platform and add-on acquisitions, minority buyouts under put options (liability SEK 1,740m at June 2026) and earn-outs (SEK 80m), less disposal proceeds. FY2026 SEK 650m (SEK 204m in H1 on FactSet's line plus the July and October deals and buyouts, less small Perfect Hair proceeds); FY2027 SEK 1,850m (SEK 1.6bn of new deals, SEK 250m of buyouts); FY2028 SEK 2,100m; FY2029 SEK 2,300m; FY2030 SEK 2,500m. At this cadence net debt rises from about SEK 9.1bn at end-2026 to about SEK 12.2bn at end-2030 while leverage falls from 2.3x to about 2.05x, leaving SEK 1.5bn to SEK 2.5bn of unused capacity to the 2.5x ceiling by 2028.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

The company gives no annual guidance by policy. Its mid-term targets, set at the 2021 listing and restated at the November 2024 Capital Markets Day: average annual adjusted EBITA growth of 15% for 2025 to 2027; adjusted EBITA margin above 10% over a rolling three-year cycle; organic growth above GDP plus 1 to 2 points; adjusted cash conversion above 70%; interest-bearing net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 2.0x to 3.0x (the CFO's ambition is below 2.5x); dividend 15% of net earnings. 2025 delivered -3% EBITA growth and a 9.4% margin. Qualitative steers since the Q2 2026 report: "cautiously optimistic" on H2 2026, solid Industry order books, a seasonally weaker Q3 with customer restraint in Trade, acquisition spending to "step up quarter by quarter", "at least 10%" margin "reasonable, especially if the economic environment improves", interest savings of about SEK 20m a quarter, minority buyouts and earn-outs "substantially smaller" in 2027.

My inputs against the targets: EBITA growth of about +0.5% in 2026, +10% in 2027, +11% in 2028, +9% in 2029 and +9% in 2030, so the 15% average for 2025 to 2027 is missed by a wide margin (2027 EBITA SEK 3.4bn against the roughly SEK 4.7bn the target implies); the 10% margin is reached in 2029 (9.9% in 2028), two years after the target period; organic growth of 3% to 3.5% is at the low end of GDP plus 1 to 2 points; cash conversion 70% to 80%, in line; leverage 2.05x to 2.3x, inside the range and below the CFO's ceiling; dividend at 15% to 16% of reported profit, in line. Against consensus (two brokers): revenue below in 2026 (+2.0% against +3.4%, on currency and divestments) and above from 2027 (acquisitions); EBITDA margin slightly above (12.4% to 12.9% against 12.2% to 12.4%); adjusted EPS on DNB Carnegie's basis SEK 1.12, 1.27, 1.44 against its SEK 1.11, 1.25, 1.36; net financial items below DNB Carnegie's 6%-of-net-debt assumption; free cash flow below the FactSet consensus of SEK 2.1bn and SEK 2.4bn for 2027 to 2028 because mine is after lease payments and a working-capital build.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

Consensus (FactSet, two brokers) is on an adjusted basis: adjusted EBITDA and adjusted EBITA exclude items affecting comparability (SEK 65m Perfect Hair capital loss in Q1 2026, SEK 28m in Q2 2026 of earn-out remeasurement and central restructuring, SEK 920m impairment in 2024) and sit after IFRS 16; brokers' EBITDA for 2025 was SEK 4,144m against FactSet's operating EBITDA of SEK 3,798m. EBIT in consensus (SEK 2,380m for 2026) is after amortisation of acquired intangibles (about SEK 690m); DNB Carnegie's EBIT margin of 7.0% for 2026 therefore corresponds to an adjusted EBITA margin of about 9.2%. The EPS lines are on two different bases and must not be averaged: DNB Carnegie's adjusted EPS (SEK 1.11 for 2026) adds back amortisation of acquired intangibles after tax (about SEK 0.31 a share) and items affecting comparability; Nordea's SEK 0.77 is after amortisation, consistent with the company's own "adjusted diluted EPS" (SEK 0.36 for H1 2026) plus a seasonally stronger second half. FactSet's EPS median of SEK 1.03 and mean of SEK 0.98 mix the two and should not be used; FactSet's consensus net income of SEK 1,309m for 2026 is on the after-amortisation basis (SEK 0.78 a share) while the EPS median is not. The model's EPS, computed from EBITDA less depreciation excluding acquired intangibles, net financial items and tax, is on DNB Carnegie's basis, and my cross-check EPS below is on that basis. Reported EPS (after amortisation of about SEK 690m to SEK 950m and items affecting comparability of SEK 125m in 2026 and SEK 60m a year thereafter, at a 25% tax rate with the Perfect Hair loss non-deductible) is about SEK 0.76, 0.91, 1.05, 1.16, 1.28 for 2026 to 2030, roughly two-thirds of adjusted EPS throughout. Free cash flow: FactSet's definition (operating cash flow less fixed-asset capital spending) gave SEK 1,732m for 2025 and a consensus of SEK 1,541m, 2,087m, 2,410m for 2026 to 2028; my cross-check deducts capital spending including intangibles and lease payments, so it is structurally about SEK 0.5bn to SEK 0.6bn lower than the FactSet definition for the same cash generation. Net debt: the company's interest-bearing net debt (SEK 9,896m at June 2026, 2.4x adjusted EBITDA) includes lease liabilities and matches FactSet's net debt of SEK 9,924m; FactSet's 2.65x uses operating EBITDA.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

Cross-check FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Earnings per share 1.12 1.27 1.44 1.58 1.73
Free cash flow 1,580 1,720 1,850 2,040 2,360

Earnings per share is adjusted, on DNB Carnegie's basis: before amortisation of acquired intangibles and items affecting comparability, after net financial items, tax at 25.5% then 25% and minority interests of SEK 10m to SEK 20m, on about 1,678m shares. Reported EPS on the same forecasts is about SEK 0.76, 0.91, 1.05, 1.16, 1.28. Free cash flow is operating cash flow after interest and tax, less capital spending including intangibles, less lease payments, before acquisitions, minority buyouts and dividends; it rests on operating cash flow of about SEK 2.75bn in 2026 (after a first half of SEK 653m), 2.98bn, 3.19bn, 3.46bn and 3.87bn.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), a Swedish serial acquirer and owner of about 114 small and medium-sized businesses in three business areas: Industry (industrial technology, automation and product solutions; SEK 14,319m of 2025 net sales), Trade (business-to-business distribution and brand owners in professional products, health and beauty and consumer products; SEK 9,588m) and Services (infrastructure services exposed to construction, and business services; SEK 9,232m); group net sales SEK 33,097m and adjusted EBITA SEK 3,117m (9.4% margin) in 2025 per the annual report of April 2026; listed on Nasdaq Stockholm since 2021; Q3 2026 report on 23 October 2026.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Lifco AB (LIFCO.B-SE), the reference for a self-funded acquisition engine run for a decade at low leverage; Indutrade AB (INDT-SE) and Addtech AB (ADDT.B-SE), Swedish serial acquirers that kept acquiring through the 2023 to 2025 downturn; Sdiptech AB (SDIP.B-SE), the closest precedent for a balance-sheet reset (eleven divestments in 2025 to 2026, leverage reduced) followed by an acquisition restart; Volati AB (VOLO-SE), the control case where leverage after the Salix spin-off in 2026 stopped the acquisition engine; Indus Holding AG (INH-DE), a German buy-and-hold holding with a stated EUR 500m acquisition programme to 2030; AddLife AB, which cut leverage from about 3.5x to about 2.2x before resuming deals; Teqnion AB and Röko AB as small Swedish acquirers whose head-office capacity has been tested by growth; Bravida and Instalco as listed references for the construction-exposed Services units. Operating conditions: a European short-cycle industrial upturn (Swedish manufacturing purchasing managers' index 58.1 and Eurozone 52.9 in September 2026); a prolonged Swedish and Nordic construction downturn (Swedish housing starts about 8,000 a year against 18,000 in 2021; Eurozone construction purchasing managers' index 42.8 in June 2026); a Riksbank rate rise expected in November 2026 and up to three European Central Bank rises priced by mid-2027; a strengthening Swedish krona with the company's currency hedging discontinued from 2026; a crowded Nordic market for acquiring founder-owned companies (new entrants such as Tången, Auroora, Röko and InstallatørGruppen); a company with a Head of M&A who has also been interim Head of Services since May 2026. Inputs: The expected change is the restart of acquisition-led growth funded from cash flow after three years of deleveraging (net debt reduced by about SEK 4bn to 2.3x to 2.4x interest-bearing net debt to adjusted EBITDA at June 2026; debt reduction "close to zero" in 2026 per the Q1 2026 call; management stating that the pace of capital allocated to acquisitions "will step up quarter by quarter"), alongside a recovery in the Services business area's margin (7.7% in Q1 2026 and 10.3% in Q2 2026 per the interim reports) and a return to organic EBITA growth (organic EBITA -4% in the first half of 2026). Test: (1) How many deals, of what size and at what multiples has Storskogen completed since the restart (LEP, Darlington EMS, Safi Equipment & Services, Verdant AS, Duratec, Hawedo and add-ons in 2025 to 2026, per the announcements), against the cadence of Lifco (about 16 to 17 deals a year), Indutrade (nine in the first half of 2026) and Sdiptech's restart, and what central M&A and integration capacity (investment managers, head-office staff of about 79 at end-2025) does each run? (2) What is the record of companies that restarted acquisitions after a reset (Sdiptech 2025 to 2026, AddLife, Indus after 2020, Storskogen itself after 2023): how fast did the deal count and acquired sales contribution rise, how many reached the pre-reset pace, and what distinguished the ones that did? (3) Does the company demonstrably have the strengths the restart needs: disciplined pricing at or below 7x EBITA (versus the up-to-10x paid in 2021 to 2022 and the SEK 920m impairment of 2024), integration without impairment, a seller reputation after the 2024 divestment of nine units, and an organisation able to run the restart and the Services turnaround at once? (4) How plausible is a Services margin recovery to the company's 10% group target given the 2023 to 2026 construction downturn, the three heads of Services in under twelve months, the workforce reduction of about 900 in 2024, and the precedents of Bravida, Instalco and Volati's construction-exposed platforms? (5) What does the company's own 2021 to 2022 acquisition vintage show about whether acquired units' earnings hold after purchase, and what do Teqnion's and Röko's cohort disclosures show for the model in general? (6) What is the evidence on organic EBITA growth turning positive at companies with this mix after a short-cycle industrial turn, and how long did it last? Focus: Whether Storskogen can raise its acquisition cadence from the 2025 to 2026 level to the pace of the proven peers while repairing its Services business area, on the strengths it has demonstrated since 2023 (cash conversion, deleveraging, price discipline on small deals), rather than on strengths not yet evidenced in its record (integration at scale, a stable Services leadership, sustained organic profit growth), and how often companies in a comparable reset achieved both at once.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Lifco AB, Indutrade AB and Addtech AB as the proven Swedish compounders with long records of outcome dispersion through cycles; Sdiptech AB as the reset-and-restart precedent; Volati AB as the leveraged, construction-exposed control case (S:t Eriks, Tornum); Indus Holding AG as a German buy-and-hold peer with industrial manufacturing exposure; Teqnion AB and Idun Industrier for small-acquirer outcome ranges; Bravida and Instalco for the dispersion of construction-exposed installation margins in the 2023 to 2026 downturn. Operating conditions: a European short-cycle industrial turn that the market expects to plateau by late 2026; a Swedish construction downturn with no confirmed turn; rising Nordic and European policy rates into 2027; a stronger Swedish krona with no currency hedging at the company from 2026; a crowded Nordic acquisition market with entry multiples reported by expert sources at 8x to 10x EBITDA; a late-2027 bank and bond refinancing (Bond 1 of SEK 1,250m at STIBOR plus 375 basis points per the Q2 2026 interim report). Inputs: The drivers that would separate the next 18 months to four years from 2022 to 2025 are: (1) acquisition-led growth replacing divestment-led shrinkage (reported revenue growth of +5%, -5% and -3% in 2023 to 2025 per FactSet; DNB Carnegie's published assumption of a 5% annual sales contribution from acquisitions ramping through 2027 and 2028; DNB Carnegie's statement that acquisitions are not normally reflected in consensus); (2) a Services margin recovery from the 2026 trough (7.7% in Q1 2026); (3) Industry operating leverage on volume (margin 11.0% in Q2 2026 from 10.4%; incremental margin reported in the interim report); (4) the fall in net financial items after the 2025 refinancings (SEK 277m in the first half of 2026 against SEK 420m a year earlier per the Q2 2026 interim report), with a company-disclosed sensitivity of SEK 69m of pre-tax profit per 100 basis points (2025 annual report); (5) a Trade margin effect from the stronger krona with hedging discontinued (transaction effects of about SEK 51m on 2025 operating profit per the annual report; about 5 percentage points of Trade's organic EBITA decline in Q1 2026 per the call). Test: how concentrated are these drivers (what share of the forecast change rests on the deal cadence and on Services alone); how wide was the dispersion of twelve-month EBITA outcomes for comparable diversified acquirers in a short-cycle upturn combined with a construction downturn (2010 to 2011, 2017 to 2018, 2024 to 2026); which events widened the range (rate shocks, impairments, control failures, currency moves, a seasonal Q1 miss) and which narrowed it; how often forecasts that rested on an acquisition cadence not yet demonstrated were met; what the sell-side's quarterly forecast errors have been for Storskogen since 2023 (beats of 2% to 8% in Q3 2024, Q4 2024, Q3 2025 and Q2 2026; a miss in Q1 2026 per DNB Carnegie and Modular Finance) and for the peers; and what the dispersion of quarterly Services margins has been (7.7% to 11.1% within twelve months). Focus: How wide and how skewed the range of Storskogen's adjusted EBITA and adjusted EPS outcomes is over the next 18 months if the forecast rests mainly on two drivers, the acquisition cadence and the Services margin, and what the historical record of comparable companies says about the probability that both drivers deliver in the same period.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Lifco AB (reports late October 2026), Indutrade AB, Addtech AB (reported 22 October 2026), Sdiptech AB, Volati AB, Beijer Alma, Indus Holding AG (reports 12 November 2026), Teqnion AB, Röko AB, Lagercrantz Group; and for the construction-exposed units Bravida, Instalco and Veidekke (market update 21 October 2026). Operating conditions: the Q3 2026 report on 23 October 2026 and the subsequent quarterly reports through early 2028 (full-year 2026 in February 2027; full-year 2027 in February 2028, which closes the company's 2025 to 2027 target period); a seasonally weak third quarter with management flagging "customer restraint" in Trade (Q2 2026 report); a Riksbank decision in November 2026; the short-cycle industrial turn against a Swedish construction downturn; a stronger krona; the late-2027 refinancing; thin sell-side coverage (two brokers visible in FactSet, DNB Carnegie and Nordea, with 2026 adjusted EPS forecasts of SEK 1.11 and SEK 0.77 and target prices of SEK 15 and SEK 16 as at 20 October 2026). Inputs: Storskogen gives no annual guidance; its mid-term targets are average adjusted EBITA growth of 15% for 2025 to 2027, an adjusted EBITA margin above 10%, organic growth above GDP plus 1 to 2 points, cash conversion above 70% and leverage of 2.0x to 3.0x (Capital Markets Day, November 2024); 2025 delivered -3% EBITA growth and a 9.4% margin. Test: (1) the record of the company's qualitative steers ("cautiously optimistic", "step up quarter by quarter", "at least 10% is reasonable") against outcomes since 2023, and whether management has ever rebased, withdrawn or reset a target; (2) what the Q3 2026 and subsequent prints need to show for the 15% target to be retained, conditioned or replaced, and the likelihood of new targets or a capital markets day in 2027 to 2028; (3) where consensus sits against the company's run-rate (FactSet consensus revenue of SEK 34,219m for 2026 and SEK 36,022m for 2027; EBITDA SEK 4,174m and SEK 4,400m; first-half 2026 revenue SEK 16,712m and adjusted EBITA SEK 1,503m), and whether the two brokers' EPS forecasts are on the same basis with respect to amortisation of acquired intangibles; (4) the revision record (FactSet: FY2026 EPS consensus down 18.5% over twelve months and flat over one month; FY2027 EBITDA down 4% over twelve months; target price down 11% over three months; buy share up 20 points to 80%) and what has historically turned revisions positive for this company and for Sdiptech, Lifco and Indutrade after a reset or a cyclical trough; (5) what peers' latest results and guidance (Addtech 22 October 2026, Lifco, Indutrade, Sdiptech, Volati, Veidekke) imply for Storskogen's Industry, Trade and Services areas; (6) how the sell-side treats announced acquisitions (DNB Carnegie: "not normally reflected" in consensus) and how quickly estimates moved after each 2025 to 2026 announcement; (7) the seasonality of beats and misses (Q1 and Q3 against Q2 and Q4) and what each coming quarter would need for a beat, a miss or an in-line print. Focus: Whether the estimate cycle at Storskogen has bottomed, what each of the next six quarterly reports would need to show for consensus and the net breadth of revisions to turn higher or lower, and how the company's unquantified targets and the two brokers' different EPS bases affect the reading of "guidance met or missed" at each event.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Lifco AB, Indutrade AB, Addtech AB, Lagercrantz Group, Sdiptech AB, Volati AB, Teqnion AB, Röko AB, Indus Holding AG, Idun Industrier, Bufab and OEM International (the Swedish serial-acquirer cohort that sell-side and investor commentary group together), and Bravida and Instalco for the construction-exposed narrative. Operating conditions: a market narrative that favours repaired or newly lit acquisition engines in a short-cycle industrial turn; Swedish compounder multiples described as hostage to the gap between their earnings yield and swap rates (DNB Carnegie, October 2026); a Riksbank hike expected in November 2026; thin and concentrated sell-side coverage (DNB Carnegie covers Storskogen and all its listed Swedish peers; Nordea is the only other visible broker); a January 2026 short-seller report on Lifco and Volati's share-price collapse after the Salix spin-off as sector sentiment events; the Q3 2026 report on 23 October 2026. Inputs: The dynamics to test for how well known they are: (1) the restart of the acquisition engine funded by cash flow rather than debt, and the mechanism by which each completed deal is an upgrade to consensus that models only owned businesses; (2) the step-down in net financial items after the 2025 refinancings and its share of 2026 earnings growth; (3) whether the two covering brokers' EPS forecasts (DNB Carnegie SEK 1.11 and Nordea SEK 0.77 for 2026 as at October 2026) are on the same basis with respect to amortisation of acquired intangibles, and how consensus compilers combine them; (4) the Services business area's margin trough (7.7% in Q1 2026) and leadership turnover (three heads in under twelve months) as the main risk to the 10% margin target; (5) Industry's operating leverage in the short-cycle turn (organic sales +6% and margin 11.0% in Q2 2026) and the market's expectation of a restock plateau by late 2026; (6) the Trade area's unhedged exposure to the krona (hedging discontinued from 2026) and the margin effect of a stronger krona with a two-to-four-quarter lag; (7) the rise in minority put option liabilities (SEK 1,740m at June 2026 from SEK 1,473m at end-2025) and amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 175m in Q2 2026) as balancing forces on reported earnings and cash; (8) the absence of an internal audit function across the business units and the 2024 impairment of SEK 920m as control and earnings-quality risks; (9) the 15% EBITA growth target for 2025 to 2027 (2025 outturn -3% per the annual report), whether commentators expect it to be met, and the prospect of new targets; (10) the leverage headroom to the chief financial officer's 2.5x ceiling and the unused SEK 1,337m buyback authorisation as capital-allocation options. Focus: Which of these dynamics are already part of the standing narrative in sell-side notes (DNB Carnegie, Nordea, Redeye, Carnegie sector reports) and investor commentary (Swedish investor forums, Twitter, podcasts, fund letters) on Storskogen and the Swedish serial-acquirer cohort, which are discussed only by one house or commentator, and which, in particular the basis of the brokers' EPS forecasts, the leverage headroom and the Services leadership gap, are not discussed at all.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), what will the Q3 2026 report of 23 October 2026 and the subsequent earnings call show on each of the following, and how does each compare with the preceding four quarters and with the sell-side previews published before the report: organic sales and organic adjusted EBITA growth by business area (Industry, Trade, Services); the adjusted EBITA margin by business area; order book commentary in Industry and any sign of a restock plateau; price pressure and the margin in Infrastructure Services; the transaction and translation currency effects on Trade; net financial items and the interest run-rate; quarterly and rolling cash conversion and the working-capital movement; acquisitions announced or completed in the quarter, their sales and multiples, and management's statement on the pipeline and cadence; the leadership of the Services business area; and any change in wording on the mid-term targets?

Request: OWN-2
