Layer 3 and Layer 4

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months
and Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

10 questions in 4 groups · 12,093 words · written 23 October 2026, 13:49 to 14:06 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Storskogen (STOR.B-SE): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what guidance, the sell-side and the narrative already reflect

Written 2026-10-23, the day of the Q3 2026 report. Money in SEK unless stated. The first and second sets were written on 22 October, before the report; the AlphaSense reports read for this set carry the report, the earnings call and DNB Carnegie's note of 20 October, so several of the earlier sets' hypotheses can now be tested against a print.

Orientation for the Consumer (read before the answers)

What the Q3 2026 report changed. Net sales SEK 8,330m (+4%; +3% organic, positive in all three business areas); adjusted EBITA SEK 806m (+6% from SEK 759m), margin 9.7% (9.5%), against DNB Carnegie's preview of SEK 764m, a beat of about 5%; organic adjusted EBITA +2%, the second consecutive positive quarter, with the nine-month figure still -2%. By area: Industry SEK 3,648m (+6%), margin 10.0% (9.5%), nine-month organic sales +5% and organic EBITA +8%, order book "solid, stable for several quarters", Product Solutions still under-utilised; Trade SEK 2,332m (flat), margin 9.9% (9.0%), Professional Products strong, health and beauty weak; Services SEK 2,360m (+7%), margin 10.7% (10.8%), EBITA +5% driven by Business Services and acquisitions, organic sales negative (all of it Infrastructure Services) and nine-month organic EBITA -16%, "early signs of improvement" in Sweden. Net financial items SEK 171m (SEK 138m a year earlier) on currency translation and lower interest income; nine months SEK 473m (SEK 633m). Reported EPS SEK 0.18 (0.19). Cash conversion 79.8% in the quarter, 73.7% rolling. Interest-bearing net debt SEK 10,420m, up SEK 524m in the quarter because SEK 738m was paid to buy out minorities; leverage 2.5x (2.3x in June); put-option liabilities down to SEK 1,086m from SEK 1,740m; management expects leverage "toward the lower end" of 2.0x to 3.0x by year-end and minority commitments to be "substantially smaller" from 2027. Acquisitions: four in the quarter (Verdant and three Services add-ons, SEK 172m of sales, SEK 26m of EBITA), ten in the year to date (SEK 591m of sales), seventeen since the restart in the second half of 2025 (SEK 950m of annual sales at "6.5x to 7.0x" EBITA), two more in October (Duratec, Hawedo, SEK 205m of sales). Targets unchanged; the chief executive said organic profit growth is "a prerequisite" for the 15% target. DNB Carnegie (20 October, before the print) cut adjusted EPS 2%, kept Buy and cut the target to SEK 15.0 from SEK 16.0.

The model summary's cross-check gaps, explained. Earnings per share: the model is SEK 0.01 to 0.02 above the Analyst's cross-check in every year; the gap is the SEK 10m to 20m of minority interests in the cross-check that the model does not carry (Richard's ruling of 16 October) plus rounding, and is immaterial on its own. Free cash flow: the model is SEK 109m above the cross-check in 2026 and SEK 97m, 117m, 142m and 163m below it in 2027 to 2030. The 2026 gap is the Analyst's conservatism on second-half operating cash flow (SEK 2.75bn for the year after a first half of SEK 653m) against the model's arithmetic (net income plus depreciation less a SEK 87m working-capital build); the nine-month actual of SEK 1,299m with the seasonally strong fourth quarter supports SEK 2.7bn to 2.9bn, so neither figure is wrong and I do not change the mechanism. The 2027 to 2030 gaps have one cause: the working-capital input (a year-end ratio of 23.2% falling to 22.0%) applied to total revenue including acquired revenue builds SEK 350m to SEK 615m a year through operating cash flow, whereas the acquired units' working capital (about 22% of SEK 1.3bn to 2.2bn of acquired annual sales, SEK 300m to 500m a year) is paid for inside the acquisitions line and must not be built a second time; the Analyst's cross-check implicitly used the smaller organic build. That input is wrong by the double count and I lower the ratio path to 23.2%, 22.5%, 21.9%, 21.4% and 21.0% (inputs-changed.md), which reconciles the model to the cross-check within 1% to 4% in every year. The Consumer should read the lower ratio as a modelling device, not as a forecast of working-capital efficiency.

Other inputs I change, all from the Q3 print (full lines in inputs-changed.md): revenue growth 2026 to +2.9% (nine-month sales SEK 25,041m plus a fourth quarter of about +3.5% as the July and October deals consolidate), with the Q3 column set to the actual (+4.4% on FactSet's base, EBITDA margin 12.7% on the brokers' basis) and Q4 to +3.5% and 12.7%; net financial items to SEK 630m, 620m, 640m, 665m and 690m (nine months SEK 473m already, net debt about SEK 0.8bn above the second set's path at year-end, the November Riksbank move); acquisitions less disposals 2026 to SEK 1,300m (SEK 204m in the first half, SEK 738m of buyouts and about SEK 150m of deals in the third quarter, Duratec and Hawedo in the fourth). The EBITDA margins, depreciation, tax, dividend, capital spending, leases and the 2027 to 2030 acquisition cadence are unchanged. On the revised inputs the forecasts of record become approximately: revenue SEK 34.1bn, 36.2bn, 39.1bn, 42.2bn, 45.4bn; adjusted EBITA SEK 3.18bn (9.3%), 3.47bn (9.6%), 3.87bn (9.9%), 4.23bn (10.0%), 4.59bn (10.1%); adjusted EPS SEK 1.13, 1.28, 1.44, 1.58, 1.73; free cash flow SEK 1.6bn, 1.7bn, 1.85bn, 2.0bn, 2.3bn. The earnings path is unchanged; the cash path now matches the Analyst's.

Two cautions on reading the model summary. First, its "against consensus" table compares the model's adjusted EBITA (before amortisation of acquired intangibles) with FactSet's consensus EBIT (after it), its net income before amortisation with a consensus net income after it, and its EPS with a median that blends two bases; the +32%, +46% and +10% it shows are basis artefacts. Like for like, the model is +1% on 2026 EBITDA against the two-broker consensus (+5% against DNB Carnegie alone), +4% on 2027 and +9% on 2028, and SEK 1.13, 1.28 and 1.44 against DNB Carnegie's SEK 1.11, 1.25 and 1.36. Second, the model's history lines for EBITDA (FactSet operating, SEK 3,798m for 2025) and EBITA (FactSet adjusted EBIT after amortisation, SEK 2,048m) sit on a different basis from the forecast inputs (brokers' adjusted, SEK 4,144m and SEK 3,117m for 2025), which is why the summary shows EBITDA growth of +10% and EPS growth of +57% for 2026: like for like the figures are +2% and +19%. The Financial Analyst should restate the 2025 history to the brokers' basis before the memo quotes any growth rate from the model.

A finding that changes the reported-EPS bridge. FactSet's 2025 pre-tax profit of SEK 1,585m at the reported 24.4% tax rate leaves SEK 1,198m, against reported net income of SEK 1,063m; the SEK 135m difference is profit attributable to non-controlling interests, which DNB Carnegie's model carries as SEK 136m for 2025 and SEK 138m, 184m and 215m for 2026 to 2028, and which the chief executive confirmed on the Q3 call when he said buying out minorities is "immediately accretive to EPS" because EPS is struck on profit attributable to the parent. The second set assumed SEK 10m to 20m, reasoning that put-option subsidiaries are consolidated as wholly owned; the put report confirms the liability treatment (remeasurement through equity, a 11.4% discount rate), but the profit allocation to minorities is real and about 11% of 2025 net profit. The consequences: on a reported basis the model's EPS is overstated by about SEK 0.08 (7%) in 2026, less thereafter as the third-quarter buyouts remove perhaps SEK 40m to 60m of the charge; against DNB Carnegie's adjusted EPS the model remains like for like, because DNB adds amortisation back gross of tax (SEK 0.41 a share) while the model adds it back net of tax (SEK 0.31), an offset of similar size. The Consumer should carry a minority charge of SEK 90m to 140m a year when presenting reported EPS and when comparing with Nordea's after-amortisation figures, and should treat the point as the first research request below.

The third set: assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks

C507 questions
D-0158D-0250D-0345D-0448D-0550D-0650D-0745

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The third set's seven questions give a consistent answer: Storskogen's forecasts for the next 18 months are more predictable than its history and less predictable than its disclosure suggests, and they are predictable in the wrong place. They are predictable on the lines the company controls and now discloses well (cash conversion, net debt and its direction, the finance line within SEK 50m, the dividend, the deal count and the sales and EBITA of each vintage), and on the Industry business area, whose short cycle is visible in European indices and in the company's own order-book commentary and whose incremental margin has now been printed twice at 19% to 21%. They are unpredictable on the two drivers that account for three-quarters of the growth the forecasts add (the acquisition cadence at two to three times the demonstrated rate, and the recovery of construction-exposed Services and Product Solutions, which the print shows has not begun, with nine-month organic EBITA in Services at -16%), and both of those drivers answer to the same external variables, Swedish rates and construction, which are tightening. The earlier sets are congruent on all of this; where they jarred (the size of the balance-sheet headroom, the cash leaving for legacy obligations, the minority leakage, the EPS bases) the print and the definitions research have settled the points and the forecast inputs have been corrected, leaving the earnings path unchanged and the cash path aligned with the Analyst's cross-check. The cone over twelve months is -9% to +5% on 2027 adjusted EBITA, with a skew that has turned slightly positive for the next two prints because the sell-side cut two days before a beat and its implied fourth quarter is too low, and that is balanced to slightly negative thereafter because the tails (a rate-driven construction leg down, a seasonal first-quarter miss, an impairment or control failure in a group of 114 units with no internal audit) are all on the downside. The least predictable line is the one the model's return depends on most: the multiple, which is set half by the cohort's relationship to swap rates and half by Storskogen's own discount, and which the guideline fair multiple places at the top of the stock's ten-year range. For the memo, the Consumer should present the operating forecasts as well-tested and moderately predictable, the reported earnings as a bridge with three named wedges, and the return as mostly a re-rating that requires both delivery and a benign rate path.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to the earlier sets

C58

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement: the two sets are congruent on direction and on the shape of the case (balance sheet repaired, acquisition engine restarted but small, Industry in a short-cycle upturn, construction-exposed Services unrepaired, no moat, management that delivers what it controls and misses what the cycle controls), and today's print confirms that shape in every particular: organic EBITA positive for a second quarter, Industry margin up, Services organic profit still falling, deals arriving at the stated discipline and at a tenth of the scale needed, leverage back up on legacy obligations. They jar in five places, and in three of the five the forecast of record inherited the wrong side, which I have corrected. The sets are therefore more congruent than the headline difference in tone (the first set's risk-heavy framing against the second set's "the balance sheet is no longer the constraint") suggests; the residual disagreement is about the size of the spare balance-sheet capacity and the size of the minority leakage, not about the thesis.

The five places where the sets jar, and how the print resolves each.

(1) Financial leverage as an amplifier and a constraint. The first set (C-76, Risks judgement) called leverage "the largest single amplifier in the case" and the 2.5x ceiling a binding constraint; the second set (P-02, P-05) said the amplifier fades after 2026 and that at the stated cadence leverage falls to 2.1x with SEK 1.5bn to 2.5bn of unused capacity by 2028. The print sides with the first set for the next two quarters and with the second set from 2027: leverage rose to 2.5x, exactly the chief financial officer's ceiling, because SEK 738m of minority buyouts fell in one quarter where the second set had spread SEK 250m to 300m a year, and net financial items rose to SEK 171m. The second set's structural point stands (the buyouts were legacy obligations, not new deals; the put liability is now SEK 1,086m, and management says the remaining commitments are much smaller from 2027), but its "unused capacity" does not exist until year-end deleveraging and will be SEK 0.5bn to 1.0bn in 2027, not SEK 1.5bn to 2.5bn, at my revised net-debt path. I disagree with the second set's framing that the capacity is "the identified upside" over 18 months; it is the shock absorber, and the upside case over 18 months rests on cadence, not on balance-sheet room.

(2) The cash leaving the company for legacy obligations. The second set's 2026 acquisitions-less-disposals input of SEK 650m was incongruent with its own put-option discussion (SEK 1,740m outstanding, windows for the 2021 to 2022 vintage falling in 2025 to 2027); the actual outflow for 2026 will be about SEK 1.3bn. Corrected.

(3) Minority interests. The second set's SEK 10m to 20m and the first set's "small in the income statement" both miss the SEK 135m charge the 2025 accounts carry (orientation above). Neither set is internally contradicted, but both the reported-EPS bridge and the dividend arithmetic (15% of profit attributable to the parent) were a few percent too generous; I have flagged it rather than changed an input because the model does not carry the line.

(4) The 2026 Services margin. The first set's steer was to model the Services recovery "later and smaller than management implies"; the second set modelled 9.0% for 2026, which looked consistent. The print shows 10.7% in the quarter and 9.6% for nine months, so 2026 will be about 9.7%, above the second set; but the nine-month organic EBITA of -16% says the underlying construction units are doing worse than either set assumed and the margin is being held by Business Services, Safi and selective bidding. Both sets were right about the mechanism and wrong about the arithmetic in opposite directions; the net effect on group EBITA is small (about SEK 50m) and is inside the unchanged 2026 margin input.

(5) The consensus. The first set treated the FactSet median EPS as a market view and read 2027 EPS growth of 21%; the second set showed the median blends DNB Carnegie's pre-amortisation basis with Nordea's post-amortisation basis. The AlphaSense definitions report confirms the second set in detail: DNB Carnegie's adjusted EPS adds back the full SEK 683m of amortisation, the company's own "adjusted diluted EPS" (SEK 0.13 and SEK 0.23 for the first two quarters) does not, and Nordea's SEK 0.77 sits on the company's basis. The second set is right, and the first set's C-49, C-67 and C-73 should be read with the 21% replaced by 12.6% on one basis. The model summary's own "against consensus" table repeats the first set's error in a different form (orientation above).

Where the sets disagreed and the print has settled it: operating leverage (C-75 against P-01). Industry's third-quarter incremental margin was 19% (EBITA up SEK 37m on SEK 198m of extra sales), the second set's 18% to 21%, not the first set's "close to proportional" 9%; Services' organic EBITA of -16% on positive organic sales is the same mechanism in reverse. The second set's reading is the one to model.

What is congruent and reinforcing, which is most of it. Both sets find no moat and a price-taker in construction, and the print's Infrastructure Services commentary ("intense industry-wide price pressure", organic sales negative) is the third consecutive quarter of confirmation. Both find Industry to be the engine of the organic turn, and the print gives a 10.0% margin and +8% nine-month organic EBITA. Both find management reliable on cash, debt and discipline, and the print gives 80% cash conversion, 6.5x to 7.0x paid, no annual guidance and unchanged targets. Both treat the 15% target as unreachable, and the chief executive's "prerequisite" remark is as close to conceding it as this management comes. Both expect the Services leadership gap to persist; it does (Ekström still interim and still Head of M&A). Both expect the estimate cycle to have bottomed; the beat against DNB Carnegie's preview makes an upgrade this week more likely than a cut. The one quantitative test the second set set for itself, organic EBITA turning positive in the second half of 2026, has been passed one quarter early.

What the congruence does not cover, and the Consumer should hold as open: the price-volume split in organic growth (the first set's "non-diagnostic" warning is still unanswerable; the pricing report confirms that only Industry has demonstrated pass-through and that the company stopped giving the split after 2022); the integration quality of the 2026 vintage (no evidence either way; the deals are months old); and the eventual home of the minority charge once the remaining puts are settled.

D-02

Does past and present delivery make the expected change plausible, and does the thesis rely only on demonstrated strengths

C50

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement: the expected change is plausible in its modest form (adjusted EBITA growing 8% to 10% a year from 2027, the margin reaching 10% in 2029, acquired sales of 4% to 5.5% a year) and implausible in management's form (15% a year averaged over 2025 to 2027), and roughly two-thirds of the modest form rests on strengths the business has demonstrated since 2023, one-third on strengths it would still need to acquire. Demonstrated, and now re-demonstrated in the print: cash generation (80% conversion in the quarter, above 70% every year since 2023), deleveraging and refinancing (SEK 4bn of net debt removed, maturities to 2027 to 2030), price discipline on small deals (6.5x to 7.0x on seventeen deals, corroborated by what the peers pay: Lifco 6.5x on seventeen deals in 2025, InstallatørGruppen about 5x, Tången 4.8x, Auroora 5.0x to 5.3x), bilateral sourcing through existing units (Hawedo came through Båstadgruppen's commercial relationship, the Services add-ons through platforms), Industry's cost pass-through and utilisation leverage (margin 10.0% from 9.5%, nine-month organic EBITA +8%), and the willingness to walk away from bad volume in Services (sales -14% in Q1 2025 to protect margin). Not demonstrated: a deal cadence two to three times the current one (SEK 950m of acquired annual sales in about fifteen months is 2.3% of sales a year against the 4% to 5.5% the forecasts need from 2027); integration of non-Nordic platforms at scale (Darlington, Duratec, Safi, Verdant are all under a year old, and the only documented integration record is the 2021 to 2022 vintage's SEK 920m impairment); a permanent Services leadership and a Services organic profit recovery (organic EBITA -16% for nine months under an interim head who also runs M&A); and sustained organic profit growth (two quarters, the first at +2%, is a start rather than a record). The thesis in the forecasts of record is built so that the undemonstrated strengths account for the upper half of the growth, which is the right way round; the thesis in management's targets is built on them almost entirely.

How the record maps onto each required strength, and the weight I give it. Cash and discipline (demonstrated, high weight): the four years of delivery on the lines management controls are the strongest evidence in the file, and the print extends them; this is what makes the self-funding loop credible at SEK 1.6bn to 2.3bn a year of spend. Price discipline (demonstrated, high weight, with a caveat): the 6.5x to 7.0x is management's own figure, undisclosed deal by deal, but the peers' disclosed multiples and the deal-flow report's finding that founder-owned companies with EBITA of SEK 20m to 50m "frequently clear at or below 7x" outside auctions make it credible; the caveat is adverse selection (the first set's C-02), which the small average size of the deals (SEK 56m of sales each against Lifco's SEK 96m) hints at. Cadence (not demonstrated, medium weight): the count is respectable (seventeen deals, comparable with Lifco's seventeen in 2025) but the sales per deal is small, and the organisation that must triple the volume is the same one that lost a Services head in three months and whose M&A head is doing two jobs; management's statement that minority commitments fall from 2027 and that cash is redirected to deals is a capacity statement, not a delivery statement. Integration (not demonstrated, low weight): the deal-flow report found no evidence either way on whether the 2020 to 2022 vintage's earnings held after purchase, because the company does not disclose it; the only group-level proxy, organic EBITA, has just turned positive. Services recovery (partly demonstrated): the segment can hold a double-digit margin on a shrinking Infrastructure base by pruning and by growing Business Services, which the print shows; it has not shown it can recover Infrastructure profit, which the forecasts need from mid-2027.

The precedents, and which side Storskogen is on. Companies that reset and restarted: Sdiptech (eleven divestments, leverage 3.3x to 2.8x, organic growth 13% in Q3 2026, estimates raised 5% in October), AddLife (3.5x to 2.2x before resuming), Indus after 2020 (a EUR 500m programme to 2030 at 7x to 7.5x); the control case is Volati, where post-spin-off leverage of 4.5x stopped the engine and the shares fell 76%. What distinguished success was leverage held below about 2.5x and deals funded from cash, and on that criterion Storskogen is on the right side, just: 2.5x today, about 2.3x by year-end on management's own expectation, 2.1x to 2.2x through 2027 on my cadence. What the precedents do not show is a company doing the restart and a segment turnaround at once with an interim head doing both; Sdiptech rebuilt its business-area layer with external hires before it restarted, and Storskogen has not. The plausibility report's overall verdict of "medium" is right, and its low confidence on the Services turnaround is the right place to put the doubt.

Where I come out against management's version. The 15% average requires 2027 adjusted EBITA of about SEK 4.7bn; the revised forecasts give SEK 3.47bn and the bull case SEK 3.65bn. The chief executive has now said organic profit growth is a prerequisite, which it is, and it has just restarted from -12% in the first quarter; no reading of the record makes 15% plausible for the target period, and the Consumer should expect the target to be re-based (I-01).

D-03

How diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers of the difference between the forecasts and the last four to six years

❌ D45

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement: concentrated. The difference between 2022 to 2025 (adjusted EBITA flat at SEK 3.1bn to 3.2bn, EPS set by the finance line) and 2026 to 2030 (adjusted EBITA +8% to 11% a year from 2027, adjusted EPS +12% a year) rests on two operating drivers that together account for three-quarters of the EBITA growth and one financial driver that accounts for most of 2026's EPS growth and then stops: (1) the acquisition cadence, about 55% to 60% of the EBITA added between 2025 and 2028; (2) the recovery of the two under-utilised pockets, construction-exposed Services and Product Solutions in Industry, about 20%; (3) the interest step-down, about two-thirds of 2026's EPS growth and nothing thereafter. The remaining quarter is diffuse and small (Industry pricing and utilisation, Trade's krona windfall and B2B mix, pruning, procurement). Concentration is a mixed blessing for predictability: both operating drivers are observable every quarter and one of them (cadence) is controlled by management, which makes the forecast easy to test; but both are untested at the scale assumed, and they are exposed to the same two external variables, Swedish rates and construction, so a bad outcome in one is correlated with a bad outcome in the other.

The bridge, so the Consumer can see the concentration. Adjusted EBITA SEK 3,117m in 2025 to about SEK 3,870m in 2028 on the revised inputs, +SEK 750m. Acquired EBITA: the 2025 to 2026 vintage (SEK 950m of sales, about SEK 95m of annualised EBITA at a 10% margin, of which perhaps SEK 50m falls in 2026) plus SEK 1.6bn of 2027 spend (SEK 230m annualised at 7x) plus SEK 1.9bn of 2028 spend (SEK 270m annualised, half in the year) gives about SEK 440m to 460m in 2028, 60% of the bridge. Services and Product Solutions: a Services margin of 10.2% on SEK 11bn in 2028 against 9.7% on SEK 9.6bn in 2026, plus Product Solutions re-absorbing capacity, about SEK 120m to 160m at 25% to 35% drop-through, 20%. Industry pricing and automation utilisation, Trade's currency windfall and mix, pruning and procurement: SEK 100m to 150m together, 15% to 20%, with the Trade currency part reversible. The interest line: SEK 828m in 2025 to about SEK 630m in 2026, worth SEK 0.09 of EPS, about 55% of the 2026 EPS increase on one basis, and flat to slightly rising afterwards.

How the print bears on each. Cadence: real, disciplined, and running at a third to a half of the rate assumed from 2027 (SEK 591m of acquired sales in nine months against the SEK 1.4bn to 2.2bn a year the forecasts need). The deal-flow report finds supply is not the constraint (German succession wave, 105 live leads at a Danish peer, 1,900 screened targets at a Swedish one, stable sub-7x pricing outside auctions), so the constraint is Storskogen's organisation and the cash freed from legacy obligations, which the chief executive says rises from 2027. Services and Product Solutions: the print shows the segment margin held (10.7%) while the organic profit of the construction units fell (nine-month organic EBITA -16%), which means the recovery driver has not started; the peers that serve the same market are at opposite ends (Bravida organic +9% and a record backlog on data centres and public works, Instalco organic +8% with Sweden +15%, against Volati's S:t Eriks -9% and Tornum -17%), and Storskogen's units behave like S:t Eriks, not like Instalco, because they are small, local and in private construction rather than in data centres or public infrastructure. Interest: SEK 171m in the quarter and SEK 473m for nine months; the step-down is banked but smaller than the second set assumed, and the November Riksbank move (25 basis points to about 2.0% on the brokers' expectation, with DNB Carnegie reading the Riksbank's own path at 2.4% by late 2027) takes back SEK 15m to 35m a year.

What the peers' dispersion says about how often forecasts resting on one or two undemonstrated drivers were met. The dispersion report's evidence: Volati missed DNB Carnegie by 18% in Q2 2026 on exactly the two drivers Storskogen depends on (construction volumes and a stalled engine); Indutrade missed by 5% to 6% in Q1 2026 on cost overruns despite a good order book; Storskogen itself beat by 2% to 9% in four of the last five quarters and missed by 6% in the fifth, the seasonal one. Forecasts that assumed an acquisition contribution not yet demonstrated were met at Sdiptech and Indutrade (both upgraded 5% to 6% in mid-2026 once deals and organic growth arrived) and not at Volati; the record is that the forecast is met when leverage allows the deals to close, and Storskogen's leverage allows it from 2027.

The quantified sensitivity, for the predictability work. A cadence of SEK 1bn a year instead of SEK 1.6bn to 1.9bn removes about SEK 110m from 2028 EBITA (3%) and about SEK 0.05 from EPS; a Services margin of 9.0% instead of 10.2% in 2028 removes about SEK 130m (3.5%) and SEK 0.06; both together, SEK 240m (6%) and SEK 0.11, which takes 2028 adjusted EPS to about SEK 1.33, DNB Carnegie's current number. The upside symmetry: SEK 3bn a year of deals and a 10.5% Services margin add about SEK 300m and SEK 0.14. The forecast therefore sits at the centre of a two-driver cone whose edges are set by the same variables, which is the definition of concentrated.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed, and so how predictable is the case

❌ D48

Question asked How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

Judgement: the cone is narrow for 2026 (nine months are printed), moderately wide for 2027 on the operating lines (adjusted EBITA SEK 3.15bn to 3.65bn around SEK 3.47bn, -9% to +5%; adjusted EPS SEK 1.12 to 1.38 around SEK 1.28), wider on reported EPS (SEK 0.60 to 0.85 around about SEK 0.72, because amortisation, minorities and items affecting comparability all sit between the two bases), and widest on the multiple, which is the line the model's return depends on most. The skew has shifted since the second set was written: it is now slightly positive for the next two prints (the Q3 beat, a fourth-quarter consensus that looks too low, an estimate cycle that has stopped falling) and balanced to slightly negative beyond them (the Riksbank path, a seasonal first quarter with a weather history, a leverage ratio at the ceiling until year-end, and three tail risks that are each low-probability and high-magnitude). Predictability is moderate on operating profit over twelve months, low on reported earnings, and low on the share price, because about three-quarters of the model's 18-month return is a re-rating that the company does not control.

The cone, line by line, to the Q3 2027 report. 2026 adjusted EBITA: SEK 3.15bn to 3.22bn (a fourth quarter of SEK 840m to 910m against SEK 816m; the risks are Trade's health and beauty units and Infrastructure Services, the supports are Industry's order book, the krona in Trade's purchasing and three newly consolidated deals). 2027 adjusted EBITA: bear SEK 3.15bn (Swedish construction takes a further leg down after the Riksbank's path, Services at 8.8%, Industry's restock plateaus at 2% organic, deals at the 2026 pace, a cold first quarter); base SEK 3.47bn; bull SEK 3.65bn (Services at 10.3% on Business Services and selective bidding, Industry holding 4% to 5% organic as the European upcycle runs its historically average fourteen quarters against six so far, SEK 2.5bn of deals). 2027 net financial items: SEK 580m to 680m (STIBOR plus 25 to 75 basis points, the Bond 1 refinancing in late 2027 at a spread between 200 and 375 basis points, currency items in the finance line as in the third quarter). 2027 adjusted EPS, on the model's basis: SEK 1.12 to 1.38. Reported EPS adds the amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 0.44 gross, SEK 0.33 net of tax), minorities (SEK 0.05 to 0.08) and items affecting comparability (SEK 0.03), so its range is SEK 0.60 to 0.85. Leverage: 2.1x to 2.5x at end-2027, with the bear case at the ceiling and an acquisition pause rather than a covenant problem (3.5x).

What widens the cone, in order of magnitude times probability. (1) The rate path and Swedish construction, which act on Services' volume, on the finance line and on the sector's multiple at once; probability of a materially worse path than the base, about one in three; magnitude SEK 150m to 250m of 2027 EBITA. (2) The first-quarter print, which has produced the company's only miss in five quarters and the stock's worst three months of the year (down 15.6% before the Q1 2026 report); probability of a miss of 5% or more, about one in three; magnitude SEK 30m to 60m in the quarter and 5% to 10% on the share price. (3) A goodwill impairment or a control failure at a unit (SEK 18bn of goodwill, no internal audit, 114 units): probability one in five over the 18 months, magnitude SEK 0.5bn to 1.5bn non-cash and a sentiment hit, no effect on adjusted EPS. (4) A currency reversal: a 5% weaker krona would cut Trade's margin gain in 2027 by SEK 30m to 50m with a lag and lift translated EBITA by about 1.5%; roughly neutral on the group, negative on Trade's quality of earnings. (5) A clustering of the remaining put exercises (SEK 1,086m): probability one in four of SEK 400m-plus in one year, magnitude a deal cadence SEK 0.3bn to 0.4bn lower rather than any earnings effect.

What narrows it. Nine months of 2026 are known. Industry's order book has been stable for several quarters and the segment is 43% of sales. The finance line's components are disclosed (floating share, swap book, maturities, the SEK 69m per 100 basis point sensitivity). The dividend, tax, depreciation and capital spending lines are mechanical. The sell-side is already below the run-rate for the fourth quarter: DNB Carnegie's pre-print 2026 adjusted EBITA of about SEK 3,056m implies a fourth quarter of about SEK 750m, 8% below last year, which is too low; the first post-print revision is therefore more likely to be up than down. And the balancing items that most worried the first set, the earn-outs and put liabilities, have shrunk by SEK 650m in a quarter.

Why the skew is now slightly positive near term and no better than balanced beyond. Near term: a beat against a stale preview, two quarters of positive organic EBITA, a fourth-quarter comparison of 9.4% that acquisitions and the krona should clear, and management guiding leverage down by year-end, all against a sell-side that cut estimates two days before the print. Beyond: the two drivers the forecast rests on (D-03) are untested at scale, the external variables that govern them are tightening (the Riksbank, Swedish construction order books down 4.8% in August, Eurozone construction in its fifty-third month of contraction), and the tails are all negative (impairment, control, refinancing in a stressed market). Over three years the skew turns positive again only if the cadence proves itself, because the spare balance-sheet capacity then becomes real.

Predictability in one line each. Operating profit, twelve months: moderate (a range of -9% to +5% on 2027 is wide but centred, and the drivers are visible each quarter). Reported earnings: low (three wedges between the bases, one of them, minorities, newly identified). Cash flow: moderate on the year, low on the quarter. Share price: low; the model's +37% to December 2027 is +5.6% earnings, +2.3% dividends and +27.5% multiple, and the multiple is set by rates and the cohort as much as by Storskogen (D-07).

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure for monitoring operating momentum and the key drivers over about the next six months

C50

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement: helpful enough to test the thesis quarter by quarter on the lines that matter most, and unhelpful on the three questions that would let the Consumer distinguish a real recovery from a cosmetic one. Helpful: quarterly net sales and adjusted EBITA by business area with organic growth in both sales and EBITA by area (the print gives Industry +8% and Services -16% organic EBITA for nine months, which is exactly the diagnostic the first set wanted), acquisitions listed with their annual sales and EBITA (SEK 172m and SEK 26m for the quarter's four deals, which gives the implied margin of the vintage), the count since the restart and the average multiple range, cash conversion on the company's definition both for the quarter and rolling, interest-bearing net debt, the leverage ratio and management's expectation for it at year-end, the put-option liability and contingent considerations with their movements, the debt maturity ladder with margins, the SEK 69m per 100 basis point rate sensitivity, and qualitative order-book commentary in Industry that has been consistent across calls. Unhelpful: no split of organic growth between price and volume (given last in 2022), no deal-by-deal multiples or purchase prices, no organic EBITA by vertical (so the Infrastructure Services decline inside Services cannot be sized from outside), no subsidiary-level results, no schedule of remaining minority buyouts, no annual guidance and no bridge to the targets, and a profit allocation to minorities that is visible only by subtraction.

The six-month monitoring plan the disclosure supports, with the figures that would confirm or refute the forecasts of record. The Q4 2026 report (early to mid February 2027) is the one scheduled event and carries most of the tests: (1) organic adjusted EBITA positive for a third quarter, and the full year at about zero (refutes if the fourth quarter is negative); (2) group adjusted EBITA SEK 840m to 910m (the forecast needs about SEK 875m; below SEK 820m restarts the cuts); (3) Services organic EBITA for the quarter, which must stop falling at double-digit rates for the 2027 margin path to hold, and Industry organic EBITA still positive with order books "solid"; (4) net financial items at or below SEK 165m for the quarter; (5) leverage at or below 2.3x and net debt at or below about SEK 10.0bn; (6) at least three acquisitions in the quarter with SEK 150m-plus of annual sales, and a statement that the cadence rises in 2027; (7) cash conversion for the year at 72% or above; (8) a dividend of SEK 0.12 or more; (9) any announcement of a permanent Head of Services or a capital markets day. Between reports, the acquisition press releases (sales and EBITA disclosed, price not) allow the cadence to be tracked monthly, and the two things the company does not control can be followed in public data: the Riksbank decision in November and its rate path, and Swedish housing starts and construction order books. Peer prints give read-across a week or two before Storskogen's: Lifco and Indutrade for Industry's short cycle, Bravida and Instalco for Swedish installation, Volati's S:t Eriks for construction materials, Veidekke's market update for public civil budgets.

What the gaps cost the Consumer, and how to work around them. The price-volume gap means the organic growth figure proves the ability to pass cost through and does not prove demand; use Industry's incremental margin (19% in the quarter) and the capacity-utilisation wording on Product Solutions as the volume proxies. The deal-multiple gap means the 6.5x to 7.0x cannot be verified; use the disclosed EBITA of each vintage against the cash paid in the cash-flow statement each half-year (the nine-month acquisitions line against SEK 591m of sales and the quarter's SEK 26m of EBITA). The vertical gap means the Infrastructure Services margin is unknowable; use the Services organic EBITA growth rate as the single proxy and treat a return to zero as the trigger. The minority gap means reported EPS cannot be rebuilt from the segment data; use pre-tax profit less tax less reported net income each quarter, as in the orientation. Against the peers, Storskogen's disclosure is in the middle of the cohort: better than Lifco (which gives no organic EBITA by area and no deal multiples either) on the organic EBITA split, worse than Sdiptech (which states an acquired-EBITA target and settles earn-outs on a disclosed schedule) and Teqnion (which discloses cohort performance) on the engine.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into operational drivers, onto revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission

C50

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement: the internal transmission is clear at the level of the business areas and of the engine, and opaque below both; the external transmission is clear in direction for every one of the five end-market and financial variables that matter, and quantifiable for only two of them (the finance line and, with a lag, Trade's currency). The chain the company itself describes is simple: cash flow funds acquisitions at 6.5x to 7.0x in five themes, the acquisitions and organic growth lift adjusted EBITA, Services' "operational excellence" and pruning take the margin above 10%, and the targets follow. Each link can be put on a line of the model: SEK 1bn of deals adds about SEK 145m of annualised EBITA and SEK 1.4bn of sales, SEK 56m of amortisation and about SEK 55m of interest if debt-funded; a point of Services margin is SEK 95m to 110m of EBITA; a point of Industry organic volume is SEK 25m to 30m at the demonstrated 19% drop-through; 100 basis points of STIBOR is SEK 69m of pre-tax profit; 5% on the krona against the euro and dollar is 50 to 100 basis points on Trade's gross margin with a one-to-four-quarter lag. What cannot be traced is the three things the targets most depend on: which verticals inside Services carry the loss, what the 2021 to 2022 vintage earns against what was paid, and how much of organic growth is price. And the last link, from adjusted EBITA to earnings per share, is where the clarity is worst, because three wedges sit between the bases (amortisation of SEK 0.41 a share gross, minorities of SEK 0.05 to 0.08, items affecting comparability of SEK 0.02 to 0.07) and the two covering brokers and the company each define "adjusted EPS" differently.

The internal chain, link by link, with what the Q3 print tells about each. "Step up the pace of capital allocated to acquisitions quarter by quarter": traceable to the quarterly deal count and the disclosed sales and EBITA of each vintage (four deals, SEK 172m, SEK 26m in the quarter; two more, SEK 205m, in October), and from there to acquired sales growth; the link to EBITA is through the undisclosed price, which the 6.5x to 7.0x claim and the peers' disclosed multiples make reasonably firm; the link to EPS is through amortisation (about 8% of the price a year on the 2025 ratio) and through the funding cost. "Adjusted EBITA margin above 10%": traceable to the three area margins (Industry 10.0%, Trade 9.9%, Services 10.7% in the quarter, group 9.7% after central costs of about SEK 40m a quarter); the group reaches 10% only if Services holds above 10.5% through a full year including the first quarter, or Industry holds 11%, and the print shows neither yet; the sub-link, Infrastructure Services' margin, is undisclosed. "15% average EBITA growth": not traceable; management has never published a bridge, and the chief executive's "organic profit growth is a prerequisite" is the first acknowledgement that the engine alone cannot deliver it. "Cash conversion above 70%, leverage 2.0x to 3.0x, dividend 15% of net profit": fully traceable and delivered. The two self-help programmes, Services standardisation and the ERP rollouts, have no stated cost, timing or benefit, so they trace to nothing until a permanent Services head gives them numbers.

The external chain, variable by variable. European short-cycle demand (Swedish manufacturing index 58.1, Eurozone 52.9, German industrial production +2.3% in August, the Bernstein short-cycle indicator at 1.08 with the upcycle six quarters into a fourteen-quarter average) transmits to Industry's order book (qualitative) and to Industry's organic sales and incremental margin (quantified each quarter); the mechanism is clear and the timing is "orders now, sales within two to three quarters". Swedish construction (housing starts about 8,000 against 18,000 in 2021, construction order books -4.8% in August, the Riksbank at about 2.0% in November and signalling 2.4% by late 2027, Trafikverket's SEK 1,171bn plan and a 48% rise in road maintenance budgets, Boverket's 52,000 homes needed against 29,000 built) transmits to Infrastructure Services' volume and price with a lag of two to four quarters (the technical-installation precedent is that service margins recover several quarters after starts); the direction is clear, the size is not, because the units' mix between private construction (weak), public infrastructure (strong) and data-centre-adjacent work (strong at Bravida and Instalco, unknown at Storskogen) is undisclosed. The krona (hedging discontinued, Trade buying in euros and dollars, about 46% of operating profit outside Sweden) transmits two ways: a stronger krona lowers Trade's purchase cost after one to four quarters (the Björn Borg precedent is six to nine months) and lowers translated EBITA at once (the third quarter's finance line carried a translation loss); quantifiable within a range. STIBOR transmits to the finance line through the disclosed SEK 69m per 100 basis points and the 11.3-month average fixed-rate period; the clearest link in the model. Input costs (steel safeguards from July 2026 with "melt and pour" from October, bitumen with oil above USD 100, Swedish construction cost inflation 3.7%, the 10% US tariff on Swedish exports) transmit to Industry's and Services' gross margins with pass-through demonstrated in Industry (one-quarter lag) and not in Services; the direction is clear, the exposure by unit is not.

The last link, to earnings per share. The model's EPS is adjusted EBITA less net financial items, less tax at 25% to 25.5%, over 1,678m shares; DNB Carnegie's adds back amortisation gross of tax and deducts minorities; Nordea's and the company's deduct amortisation and minorities. On the revised forecasts for 2027: model SEK 1.28; DNB Carnegie-basis about SEK 1.31 (after SEK 0.08 of minorities and with the gross add-back); company-basis about SEK 0.78; reported about SEK 0.73 after items affecting comparability. Four numbers for one year's earnings is the clearest statement of how unclear this link is, and the memo should present two of them (the model's and the reported) and name the bridge.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically, and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven

❌ D45

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement: the co-movement table could not be built (no price history was found for the ticker), so this answer rests on the dashboard's return and rating data, on the broker and sector evidence of what moves the cohort, and on the stock's own event history; on that evidence the level of Storskogen's multiple is set by the sector and the rate regime, and the size of its discount to the sector is set by the company, in roughly equal measure over a rolling six months. The sector-driven half: DNB Carnegie's standing finding that Swedish compounder multiples are "hostage" to the gap between their earnings yield and the ten-year euro swap rate, which tightened in the third quarter and prompted the 20 October target cut "to reflect lower peer multiples" with no change in Storskogen's estimates beyond 2%; the 2022 precedent in which the cohort's median multiple halved; the January 2026 short-seller note on Lifco and Volati's collapse, which the sector research says keep the whole cohort's risk premium high; and the cohort's own eight-month return dispersion (Indus +69%, thyssenkrupp +56%, Sdiptech +42%, Aalberts +47%, Storskogen +17%, Lifco -5%, Röko +2%, Teqnion -3%, Volati -76% over twelve months), which shows a sector that does not move as a bloc but sorts its members by balance-sheet repair and delivery, which is the company-driven half. The company-driven half: Storskogen's 24-month-forward multiple sits at the 77th percentile of its own five-year range and the 89th of the last two years because its discount to the proven compounders (9.6x against 20x to 24x EV/EBITA on DNB Carnegie's 2026 figures) has narrowed as leverage fell from 4.35x to 2.4x, and its worst three months of 2026 (down 15.6% into the Q1 miss) and its four target cuts were stock-specific. For predictability this split is unfavourable: the company can earn its way to a smaller discount, but it cannot earn its way past a rates-driven de-rating of the cohort, and the model's 18-month return is three-quarters multiple.

The evidence that the level is sector- and rates-driven. The whole cohort trades off one framework written by one house, and that framework is explicit that the swap rate sets the ceiling; when Addtech's earnings-yield spread widened in the October correction, DNB Carnegie called the cohort more attractive as a group, and when the spread tightened in September it cut targets across the group including Storskogen's. The sector research the first set used says the market this month "refuses re-ratings" and the portfolio's Fragility tilt is Double Underweight; the Riksbank's November move is a cohort event, not a Storskogen event. Jefferies' counter-view (April 2025) that the cohort's multiples would need a 200 to 300 basis point rise in corporate bond yields to compress materially is the dissent, and it applies to the proven names with scarcity premia, not to a sub-investment-grade name at 9x.

The evidence that the discount is company-driven. Within the cohort the names that repaired balance sheets re-rated (Sdiptech's leverage 3.3x to 2.8x, shares +42%; Indus with its programme funded, +69%), the leveraged name collapsed (Volati, 4.5x, -76%), and Storskogen, repaired but unproven, sits between (+17% over twelve months, +12% over six, -4.5% over nine, with the nine-month figure capturing the Q1 miss and the Lifco short-seller contagion in January). The dashboard rates its technical momentum C, its sell-side momentum D and its valuation D (P/E percentile 77 against its own ten years), all mid-to-low in the sector, which is where a repaired but undemonstrated engine should sit. DNB Carnegie's bear case (EBITA 15% below, multiple 15% below 12x) valued the stock at about SEK 11, where it trades; the market's price is the broker's bear, which is a company-specific verdict on the record.

What this means for the model's return and for the memo's fair multiple. The model's 18-month return of +37% to December 2027 is +27.5% from the multiple moving from 8.0x to the guideline 9.39x on the model's EPS, +5.6% from earnings and +2.3% from dividends. The guideline multiple is the top of the stock's own ten-year range (the dashboard's 90th percentile band is 9.40x, the median 7.60x), which is where a stock in a peak-delivery phase trades and not where a stock turning up from a trough trades; on the company-driven half of the evidence, the discount to the cohort narrows only as cadence and Services delivery are printed, which is a 2027 to 2028 event, and on the sector-driven half, a 25 to 75 basis point rise in Swedish and European rates over the same period works against the whole cohort's level. I therefore regard the model's return as resting on the least predictable line in the case, and I would steer the Consumer to present the multiple-driven part of the return as conditional on two things the company controls (cadence, Services) and one it does not (rates), and to hold the ten-year median of 7.6x as the trough case: at 7.6x on 2027 model EPS of SEK 1.28 the shares are worth about SEK 9.7, 12% below today.

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole C55 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The fourth set asks whether our assessment is already reflected in guidance, in sell-side estimates and in the narrative, and the three answers point the same way with different strengths. Guidance reflects more than our assessment, not less: the targets (15% growth, 10% margin) sit above the forecasts of record and above consensus, will be missed at their first checkpoint in February 2028, and are more likely than not to be re-based before then; the gap is unfavourable on the numbers and neutral on the qualitative steers, which the print supports, and the one guidance event in the window is a priced negative. Sell-side estimates reflect slightly less than our assessment: the two brokers are in line with us on 2026 and 2027 revenue, 1% to 4% below on 2027 EBITDA and 9% below on 2028 because their models carry no acquisitions after the ones announced, their revision cycle has bottomed and is about to turn modestly positive on a beat they did not expect, and the only likely cut in the next 18 months is the seasonal first quarter; the gap is modestly favourable, with the caveat that the finance line and the minority charge limit the EPS upgrades to a fraction of the EBITA upgrades. The narrative reflects least: it is one house's framework with no investor voice, it has the deal-as-upgrade mechanism in principle and not in price, it has just swung to "no headroom" two months before leverage falls, and it has never examined the EPS bases, the minority leakage, the control gap or the buyback authorisation; the gap is favourable on balance, because the unarticulated positives are larger than the unarticulated negatives and the misarticulated item is misarticulated against the stock. Taken together: the stock is priced at the sole broker's bear case on a blended earnings figure nobody has reconciled, with a target set everyone has discounted and an engine everyone describes and nobody forecasts. That is a favourable gap for an investment case in the next six months (the Q4 print, the deal cadence, the year-end leverage) and a moderately favourable one for months six to eighteen (the 2027 to 2028 estimates rising with each deal), bounded by three things our assessment also holds and the narrative under-weights: the Services recovery has not started, the first-quarter print is the weak link, and the return the model shows rests mostly on a multiple that the cohort's relationship to rates sets as much as Storskogen's delivery does.

Guidance

1 question
I-0140
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events, and what would drive each outcome; secondarily, the targets for 18 months to three years out

❌ D40

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement: there is no annual guidance to raise, meet or reduce, so the question resolves into three others. First, will the qualitative steers ("cautiously optimistic", "the pace of acquisitions will step up quarter by quarter", "at least 10% is reasonable", "leverage toward the lower end by year-end") be delivered at each of the five reports in the next 18 months (Q4 2026 in February 2027, Q1 2027 in April or May, Q2 in August, Q3 in October, Q4 2027 in February 2028)? Mostly yes: probability about 70% that each report can honestly repeat them, highest at Q4 2026 and Q2 2027 and lowest at Q1 2027, where weather, the Riksbank and Infrastructure Services make a softer tone likely. Second, will the mid-term targets be met at their first checkpoint, the full-year 2027 report in February 2028? The 15% average EBITA growth for 2025 to 2027 will be missed by a wide margin (probability of being met below 5%; the forecasts of record give 2027 adjusted EBITA of about SEK 3.47bn against the roughly SEK 4.7bn the target implies, and even the bull case is SEK 3.65bn); the 10% margin on a rolling basis is more likely missed than met by end-2027 (probability of being met about 25%: the forecasts give 9.6% for 2027, and the quarter-by-quarter path needs Services above 10.5% through a first quarter); the organic growth target (GDP plus 1 to 2 points, about 3.5% to 4.5%) is borderline at the current 3%; cash conversion, leverage and the dividend will be met. Third, will the targets be re-based, withdrawn or replaced? Very likely: probability about 65% that new targets or a capital markets day are announced by the Q4 2027 report, most likely alongside the Q4 2026 report or in the second half of 2027, because the company re-based once before (November 2024, after organic growth turned negative) and because the chief executive has begun conditioning the target ("prerequisite"). A re-base would be read by the market as a reduction, but it is a reduction the two covering brokers and the sector research have already discounted ("highly challenging"), so the event is a narrative negative with little estimate content.

What would drive each outcome at each event, from the drivers the earlier sets identified. Q4 2026 (February 2027): the steers are most likely repeated and the tone could firm, because the fourth quarter compares against a 9.4% margin, Industry's order book is stable, Trade has the krona working for it, three deals consolidate, and leverage should print at or below 2.3x; the risk is a one-off (a divestment loss, a remeasurement) below adjusted EBITA that spoils reported earnings, as in each of the last three years. Q1 2027: the weak quarter in every year since listing, with a 6% miss in 2026 on frost; a 25 to 50 basis point higher STIBOR in the finance line; the first quarter in which Infrastructure Services must show that selective bidding has stopped the organic profit decline; this is where "cautiously optimistic" is most likely to be softened to "mixed" and where the first-set warning about a seasonal miss applies. Q2 2027: the strong quarter, the second quarter of acquired growth above 3%, the point at which "step up quarter by quarter" is either visible in a deal count of three to five a quarter or is not; probability of a constructive report about 70%. Q3 2027: the late-2027 refinancing of Bond 1 (SEK 1,250m at STIBOR plus 375 basis points) should be announced around here, and its spread will be the clearest signal of the credit market's verdict; a spread below 300 basis points is a modest positive steer the company would repeat. Q4 2027 (February 2028): the target period closes, the miss on 15% is formalised, and the only question is whether the company arrives with new targets already published. The driver of a positive surprise across all five events is a rising deal count with organic EBITA positive, which the second set's P-10 named as the two "up" triggers and the print has delivered one of; the driver of a negative surprise is Swedish construction taking a further leg down into the Riksbank's path, which would show first in Services' organic EBITA and second in the leverage ratio.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. The replacement targets, when they come, will most probably keep 15% as a through-the-cycle growth ambition with a new base year (every Nordic compounder states 15%: Lagercrantz, Sdiptech, Volati), keep the 10% margin, and may add an organic and acquired split or an acquired-EBITA figure as Sdiptech does; that would be a re-base down in substance and a clarity gain in form. A raise of any target over three years is implausible on the evidence (probability below 10%); the one ambition that could rise is the leverage range being narrowed toward 2.0x to 2.5x as the puts run off, which would be read as conservative rather than ambitious. The reading for the memo: current guidance is not conservative, it is unreachable on one count and stretching on another, and the company's only quantified capacity statement (cash redirected from buyouts to deals from 2027) is already in the forecasts of record. The gap between our assessment and what guidance reflects is therefore unfavourable on the targets (we are below them) and neutral on the steers (we agree with them), and the one guidance event in the window, a re-base, is a priced negative.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0258
I-02

How likely are consensus forecasts and the net breadth of revisions to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events, and why; secondarily, the forecasts for 18 months to three years out

C58

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement: the estimate cycle has bottomed and is about to turn modestly positive, with the first upgrades this week and the pattern over the next 18 months being "small upgrades at the strong quarters, one cut at the weak one, outer years rising as deals are added", for a cumulative move in 2027 consensus EBITDA of +2% to +5% and in 2027 EPS of 0% to +5% (the finance line and minorities offset part of the operating upgrade), and in 2028 of +5% to +10%. The evidence for the turn: the FY2026 consensus EPS fell 18.5% over twelve months and has been flat for a month; DNB Carnegie cut 2% two days before a print that beat its adjusted EBITA by about 5% and its sales by 0.6%; its pre-print 2026 adjusted EBITA (about SEK 3,056m) implies a fourth quarter 8% below last year's, which the nine-month run-rate (SEK 2,309m, flat) and the fourth-quarter drivers (acquisitions, the krona, a 9.4% comparison) make unlikely; its 2027 and 2028 models carry zero acquisition spend and leverage falling to 1.7x, so every completed deal is an upgrade by construction, and the company closed six in the quarter and October; and its minority-interest line rises to SEK 184m and 215m in 2027 and 2028 although SEK 738m of buyouts in the quarter should cut it. The evidence against a strong turn: there are two brokers, so "breadth" is one name's decision; Nordea's note is dated 28 September and sits on a different EPS basis, so its update may lower the blended FactSet median even as it raises its own number; the finance line printed SEK 171m against a DNB Carnegie annual assumption of SEK 599m, which is a 2% EPS cut waiting to happen; DNB Carnegie's tax rate falls to 24% and 23% in 2027 and 2028 against a company that has printed 24% to 25%, another 1% to 2% of EPS at risk; and the first quarter of 2027 carries the seasonal and rate risk that produced the only cut-restarting print in the last two years.

Where our forecasts sit against the two brokers, like for like, so the Consumer can see where the upgrades would come from. Revenue: ours SEK 34.1bn, 36.2bn, 39.1bn against the median SEK 34.2bn, 36.0bn, 37.3bn, so in line through 2027 and +5% in 2028 on cadence. EBITDA (brokers' basis): ours SEK 4.22bn, 4.56bn, 5.05bn against the median SEK 4.17bn, 4.40bn, 4.64bn and DNB Carnegie's SEK 4.04bn, 4.40bn, 4.64bn: +1% (+5% against DNB Carnegie) in 2026, +4% in 2027, +9% in 2028. Adjusted EBITA: ours SEK 3.18bn, 3.47bn, 3.87bn against DNB Carnegie's about SEK 3.06bn, 3.39bn, 3.60bn: +4%, +2%, +7%. Adjusted EPS: ours SEK 1.13, 1.28, 1.44 against DNB Carnegie's SEK 1.11, 1.25, 1.36, with the basis offsets (their gross amortisation add-back, our omission of minorities) roughly cancelling; against Nordea's SEK 0.77, 1.03, 1.22 the comparison is on the reported-plus-items basis, where our equivalents are about SEK 0.72, 0.80, 0.93, so we are below Nordea in 2027 and 2028, which says Nordea's outer years assume a faster fall in the finance line or in amortisation than we do, and are the numbers most likely to be cut. The net of all this: the 2026 and 2027 operating lines get modest upgrades, the below-the-line items get modest cuts, and the outer year gets the acquisition upgrades.

Event by event. This week (post-print notes): DNB Carnegie raises 2026 adjusted EBITA 2% to 4% and 2027 1% to 2%, trims the finance line, keeps SEK 15.0; Nordea moves its own numbers up; the one-month EPS revision turns positive in November; buy share stays at 80%. Q4 2026 (February 2027): the fourth quarter beats the implied consensus by 3% to 8% on the base case, outer years rise 1% to 3% on Duratec, Hawedo and the fourth-quarter deals, the dividend prints at SEK 0.12 to 0.13; the risk is a below-the-line one-off that cuts reported-basis numbers. Q1 2027 (May): the probability of a cut of 2% to 5% to 2027 is about 40%, on weather, the Riksbank and Infrastructure Services; this is the event at which the revision count is most likely to go negative. Q2 and Q3 2027: in line to small beats, with 2028 rising as the first-half 2027 deals are added; a late-2027 refinancing spread below 300 basis points is a 1% EPS upgrade for 2028. Q4 2027 (February 2028): the target re-base dominates the narrative, the 2028 estimates by then carry whatever cadence was printed, and FactSet will have a 2029 column.

What has turned revisions positive for this company and its peers, applied here. For Storskogen itself the two precedents are Q3 and Q4 2024 (beats of 8% and 5% to 7% on cost and mix, estimates up 3%); for Sdiptech it was the completion of the clean-up followed by organic growth of 11% to 13% (estimates up 5% in October 2026); for Indutrade it was organic growth returning to 5% (estimates up 5% to 6% in July 2026); for Lifco it is each acquisition (1% to 4% in October). Storskogen has the clean-up done, the organic turn starting and the deals arriving; what it lacks is the organic growth rate of Sdiptech or Indutrade, which is why its upgrades will be small and deal-driven rather than large and cyclical. The one structural feature that works for the revisions is the one the second set identified: the sell-side models only owned businesses, so a company whose growth is 55% acquired is under-forecast by construction for as long as it keeps buying, and the sector research says that window runs 12 to 24 months after a restart.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. The 2028 consensus (DNB Carnegie SEK 1.36, Nordea SEK 1.22) assumes no new deals and a falling finance line; ours is SEK 1.44 with SEK 1.9bn of 2028 deals and a rising finance line. If the cadence prints at the base case the 2028 number rises 5% to 8% by end-2027; if it prints at the 2026 pace the number is about right; if the Services recovery fails the number is cut 3% to 5%. The skew is to upgrades, because the omission of deals is systematic and the Services risk is partly known, and because by 2028 the minority charge will have shrunk with the puts. The 2029 column, when FactSet adds it, will start from one or two brokers extrapolating 2028, which is exactly the "sharp drop-off in projected growth one to two years out" Redeye describes, and is the point at which a company with a working engine beats consensus most easily.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0362
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of sell-side commentary and investor commentary, and how well known is each

C62

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement: the sell-side narrative on Storskogen is one house's narrative (DNB Carnegie writes the only detailed research AlphaSense holds, covers every listed peer with the same framework and sets the cohort's valuation logic; Nordea's notes are not retrievable; SEB, Redeye and ABG are named as possible publishers with nothing found), and investor commentary is effectively absent (the narrative report searched Swedish investor forums, Twitter, podcasts and fund letters and cites none; the dashboard's interest level is "D, nonplussed"). Within that thin narrative the dynamics divide cleanly. Fully articulated and well known: the balance-sheet repair and the restart of acquisitions; the discount to the proven compounders and its causes (leverage, returns, the 2021 to 2022 record); the Services construction drag and the Q1 margin trough; the seasonal pattern of beats and misses; the unreachability of the 15% target; the sector's multiples being hostage to swap rates; the Lifco short-seller event and Volati's collapse as cohort sentiment markers. Articulated by one house and therefore known to the few who read it: the mechanism by which each completed deal is an upgrade because consensus models only owned businesses (DNB Carnegie states it in every note and runs a separate "5% M&A scenario"); the interest step-down and its share of earnings growth; the minority put drain and its effect on leverage (now also in the company's own report as "a milestone"); Trade's unhedged krona exposure and its lag. Not articulated anywhere: that the two brokers' EPS forecasts are on different bases and that FactSet's median and mean blend them, so the "consensus" P/E and growth rates the market quotes are not a measure of anything; that the income statement carries a SEK 135m charge for non-controlling interests that neither the sell-side's headline adjusted EPS nor the company's targets mention; the leverage headroom (the narrative now says "at the ceiling, no room", after the quarter's 2.5x, when management's own guidance is toward 2.3x by year-end and the cadence is cash-funded from 2027); the Services leadership vacuum (press releases and a line in one DNB Carnegie note; nobody treats it as the main risk to the margin target, which it is); the absence of an internal audit function across 114 units (the governance report only); the unused SEK 1,337m buyback authorisation (nobody); and the accounting of the put remeasurement through equity, which keeps a SEK 205m half-year swing out of profit and which no commentator has examined. On balance the unarticulated dynamics are more favourable to the case than unfavourable, because the two positive mechanics (the engine's under-forecasting, the headroom returning in 2027) are larger in earnings terms than the two negative ones (minorities, control), and because the one dynamic that is misarticulated (leverage "at the ceiling") is misarticulated in the pessimistic direction two months before it reverses.

How well known each of the ten dynamics is, with the evidence and the implication. (1) The cash-funded restart and the deal-as-upgrade mechanism: DNB Carnegie since 2024, explicit on 20 October ("acquisitions are not normally reflected in consensus", "the resumption of M&A is the primary driver of future earnings revisions"); the Q3 presentation and call carry the count and multiple range; well known to the sell-side, not evidenced in any investor commentary; the implication is that the mechanism is known in principle and unpriced in practice, which is the window the sector research describes. (2) The interest step-down: in the annual report, every DNB Carnegie note and the company's own "SEK 20m a quarter" statement; well known, fully priced, and now slightly over-estimated by the one house (SEK 599m for 2026 against a nine-month SEK 473m). (3) The EPS bases: DNB Carnegie discloses its own method and nothing about Nordea's; no commentator reconciles the two; the definitions report could not retrieve Nordea's, SEB's, Redeye's or ABG's models; unknown, and the consequence is that the stock screens at an 8.6x to 9.3x P/E on a blended figure that overstates the earnings the company's own accounts show by a third. (4) The Services trough and the leadership turnover: the trough is in every note; the turnover is in three press releases and one sentence of research; half known, with the risk under-weighted. (5) Industry's operating leverage and the plateau risk: DNB Carnegie's 12 August and 20 October notes, with the plateau framed as "late 2026 to late 2027" and Bernstein and Rothschild framing the upcycle as six quarters into fourteen; well known, with the market's expectation of a plateau more cautious than the macro evidence. (6) Trade and the krona: the company's Q1 2026 call and presentation, DNB Carnegie since August 2025; known to the sell-side, with the lag understood and the magnitude not quantified by anyone. (7) Put liabilities and amortisation: the puts are in the Q3 report and in DNB Carnegie's leverage commentary; amortisation is treated as an accounting artefact by the whole Nordic sell-side (DNB Carnegie's "double charge" argument of November 2025); the puts are now well known, the amortisation is known and dismissed, the minorities are not discussed. (8) Internal audit and the 2024 impairment: the impairment is well known; the control gap is in the governance report and in nobody's narrative; the Asker Healthcare downgrade of September 2026 shows this house will act on earnings-quality concerns when it finds them. (9) The 15% target: "highly challenging" in every DNB Carnegie note since September; the expectation of a re-base is in the research; well known and priced. (10) The headroom and the buyback: the narrative has moved in two months from "leverage falling to 1.7x by 2028" (DNB Carnegie's model) to "zero near-term headroom" (the narrative report's reading of the quarter); neither is right, and the buyback authorisation has never been mentioned by any commentator.

How this compares with the peers, and why it matters for the memo. For Lifco, Indutrade, Addtech and Lagercrantz the narrative is broad (several houses, Redeye, expert networks, retail investor forums), stable and about the quality of the engine; for Sdiptech and Indus it is about the restart and has turned positive as the prints arrived; for Volati it is about leverage and governance; for Storskogen it is one house's framework, polarised between "believers" in a multiple-arbitrage story and "non-believers" who think the model only worked at low rates (DNB Carnegie's own description), and thin enough that a single print moves it. A thin, one-house narrative on a repaired balance sheet with an under-forecast engine is the configuration in which the sector research says the 12 to 24 month window pays; it is also the configuration in which a seasonal miss or a control event takes 20% to 30% off the price with no change in the fundamentals, because there is no second voice. The memo should say both.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), a Swedish serial acquirer and owner of about 114 small and medium-sized businesses in three business areas: Industry (industrial technology, automation and product solutions), Trade (business-to-business distribution and brand owners in professional products, health and beauty and consumer products) and Services (infrastructure services exposed to construction, and business services); net sales SEK 33,097m and adjusted EBITA SEK 3,117m in 2025 per the annual report of April 2026; listed on Nasdaq Stockholm since 2021; Q3 2026 report published 23 October 2026.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), what did the share price do on and after the Q3 2026 report of 23 October 2026 (net sales SEK 8,330m and adjusted EBITA SEK 806m per the interim report), and what did each covering broker publish after it: DNB Carnegie (Buy, target SEK 15.0 on 20 October 2026), Nordea Equity Research (Buy, target SEK 16.0 on 28 September 2026) and any other house that publishes (SEB, Redeye, ABG Sundal Collier, Pareto, Carnegie sector reports), with each house's new forecasts for 2026 to 2028 of net sales, adjusted EBITDA, adjusted EBITA, net financial items, tax rate, profit attributable to non-controlling interests, amortisation of acquired intangibles, acquisition spending and adjusted and reported earnings per share, and a statement for each house of whether its adjusted EPS adds back amortisation of acquired intangibles before or after tax and whether it deducts non-controlling interests; and how FactSet, Modular Finance and Infront compile their consensus for this company from these differently defined figures?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), what has the profit attributable to non-controlling interests been in each quarter and year from 2022 to the third quarter of 2026 (the 2025 annual report shows pre-tax profit of SEK 1,585m and net profit attributable to the parent of SEK 1,063m), which subsidiaries and which business areas it arises from, how it is affected by the SEK 738m of minority buyouts paid in the third quarter of 2026 and by the remaining minority put-option liability of SEK 1,086m at 30 September 2026 (per the Q3 2026 interim report), on what schedule the remaining put options fall due and at what valuation formulas, and how Lifco, Indutrade, Addtech, Sdiptech and Indus Holding disclose and forecast the same line, so that the bridge from adjusted EBITA to reported earnings per share can be built with the minority charge, the amortisation of acquired intangibles and items affecting comparability each shown separately?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Storskogen Group AB (STOR.B-SE), who owns the shares and how has that changed over the last twelve months (the founders' Class A and B holdings, AMF Pension at about 9.8% of capital and Swedbank Robur per the nomination committee announcement of 22 October 2026, other institutions above 2%, retail holdings through Avanza and Nordnet, foreign holders, and short interest reported to Finansinspektionen), what Swedish and international investors, fund managers, podcasts, investor forums and social-media commentators have said about the company since the January 2026 short-seller report on Lifco and since the Q3 2026 report of 23 October 2026, which of the company's dynamics they discuss (the restart of acquisitions, the Services business area, leverage and minority buyouts, the mid-term targets of 15% adjusted EBITA growth and a margin above 10% set at the November 2024 capital markets day, the valuation discount to Lifco, Indutrade and Addtech) and which they do not, and how this compares with investor commentary on Sdiptech, Volati, Indus Holding and Lifco over the same period?
